In the town of the land of Laṅkā
Is the central mountain, Malaya,
Whose slopes and plains run down to the ocean's shore.
Go to its peak, where violence dwells!
In the domain beheld through vajra means,
Upon the lord of the charnel ground and his host
In his great haunt in the dreadful wilds,
Send forth clouds of vajra birds across the three realms!
Akṣobhya, being unshakable by anything,
Embodiment of all the buddhas,
Vajrasattva, suchness,
Vajragarbha, mother and protector of the three existences,
In the sky of great emptiness
Act as the great Blazing One in nine dances!
And having come to the ground of compassion,
Perform the conduct of the child who enters in accord!
Victors as you are,
From emptiness, the source of all within nonexistence,
And victors who have gone the pure path
In the buddhafield Ghanavyūha:
I am the servant of your command.
Since the time for the work of your command has come,
Those whom all the buddhas found hard to tame,
All of them without exception I shall tame.
Those not yet freed I shall free;
Those not yet liberated I shall liberate;
Those not yet relieved I shall relieve,
And establish them in the genuine itself.
In the gathering of all secret intentions,
Through the wondrous secret maṇḍala,
For sentient beings without limit
Bestow the blessing that rouses the essence!"

When Vajradhara had fiercely exhorted them in this way, from the palace of the dharmadhātu all the tathāgatas, the fire of their great wisdom blazing like the fire of the eon at the end of time, massed clouds of compassion to the limits of space, performed fierce rites in the nine dances of the great Blazing One, and gave command to the host of vajra horses:

"Slay! Slay utterly, everywhere!
With the nine vajra gaits of the horse,
Show the world what all its minds desire!
To the black and fierce host of the world
Who dwell in the haunts of the charnel ground,
With the bird-lamps of vajra contemplation,
Until saṃsāra has been liberated,
Wearing the armor of your vow,
Through the entry into the contemplation of great compassion,
Cut through the very limits of saṃsāra!"

Thus fiercely commanded,

Vajradharma, the Wheel of Great Joy,
Avalokiteśvara, the Vajra Supreme Eye,
The Vajra Lotus, Most Joyful,
The host of Vairocana and of Vāgīśvara,
Great Awakening, chief of thoroughbred steeds:
Through the miraculous power of nine great means
The hosts of emanated Vajra Horse-Faces
Filled all the realms of space
And arrived upon the peak of Malaya.

Until saṃsāra is emptied,
Avalokiteśvara, by the power of compassion,
And the victors of Ghanavyūha too,
Gathered as one in secret intention.
Intending the essence that sets right the crookedness of the three realms,
They bestowed the blessing that shakes out the three times for good.
Saṃsāra is the nature of reality, Avalokiteśvara;
Suffering is made into the path of awakening;
Karma itself unfolds as self-arisen wisdom;
Ripening is refuted: it is the vajra itself.
Primordially just as it is, uncontrived,
They envisioned it as the great self-arisen maṇḍala.

The Lord of Secrets, Vajradhara, too,
From his vajra body, speech and mind,
Brought forth emanated supreme sons, Vajradharas,
Youths by the tens of millions and the millions,
All like massing summer clouds,
To the boundless limits of the expanse of space...

And arrived upon the peak of Malaya.
In the guise of means that enter in accord,
Through the conduct of a child here
He performed all the deeds of Māra's domain
And showed himself as a child in the world system.

This is the twenty-sixth chapter, called the Vajra Assignment of the Four Activities, from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

Then, when that being who enters in accord
Looked with the eyes of accord,
That fierce preta of karma
Had seized things and made them his own.
These were the things of karmic ripening:
At the foot of his fortress of rotting corpses and skulls,
His encampment was ringed all around
By a swamp of blood, excrement and urine, human flesh,
Marrow, brains and brain fat,
Pus, lymph and the like.
He was encircled by a malicious retinue
Of worms and very great worms,
Flies, gadflies and bees,
Snakes, scorpions, spiders and the like,
Flocks of herons, crows, magpies and owls,
Jackals, wolves, tigers, lions and the like.
The daughters, wives and sisters
Of Karuna, general of the gods,
And the sisters, wives and daughters
Of the Great God (Śiva), lord of the world,
He had carried off and brought under his power
As his host of concubines, mistresses and slave women.
The twelve, the eight and the others,
Mātṛkās, phra men goddesses, yakṣiṇīs,
Rākṣasīs, piśācīs, blood-stealers,
Bearers of Māra's name, thieves of the life force,
Female ministers, generals and commanders,
Captains, messengers and envoys,
Each with her own support and her implements,
A retinue that obeys and awaits his command, asking "What shall we do?",
Sat ferocious, ready to be unleashed.
The eight rākṣasa daughters, the gandharvīs,
Hosts of tigers, lions, foxes, wolves and hyenas,
And flocks of vultures, ravens and owls
Sat ferocious, singing his praises.
The eight great attendants of the rākṣasa,
Gaurī, Caurī, Pramohā,
Vetālī and Pukkasī,
Ghasmarī and Śmaśānī,
Caṇḍālī and the rest,
A host of fierce and ferocious mothers,
Sat in utterly terrifying forms.
The four gatekeepers of the charnel-ground land,
The Striving One, the Faced One, the Backward Dancer,
And likewise the Entering One, these four great ones,
Hold the secret gates and guard the gates of command.
The lady of the charnel ground, the Boat-Lifter,
The Greedy One and the Utterly Greedy One,
The Drunken One and the Blood-Drinker
Sat with the glow of those intoxicated by lust.
In the center of the heaped corpses and skulls,
Firmly fixed by a great blazing nail of meteoric iron,
Was the lady of the inner chamber, the attendant Krodhīśvarī,
With a skull ladle filled with blood,
And beer mixed with grain, butter and the five substances,
Ever holding her own enjoyments.
Because she was intoxicated with lust and greedy,
She was known as the Greedy Mistress of Desire.

In order for the Blessed One to bring under his power
That encampment-town of Rudra
Through the nine dances of the great Blazing One,
The retinue of tamed generals gathered,
The mighty lords of the world.
While most of them were busy with their tasks,
The son of the great glorious Vajradhara,
Not that very reality itself,
Drawn forth from the youth's lap,
Vajrakumāra, Ucchuṣma,
Dark blue, with a wrathful frown and matted locks,
Performed the nine dances of the great Blazing One.
Exactly like the lord of the charnel ground,
He fashioned his head, hands and feet there.
Through the application of the five nectars,
He transformed the five impure substances into nectar
And purified all into the nectar of emptiness.
The female retinue, the attendant and the rest,
Through wisdom unattached to its conduct,
And activity that does not accumulate what it does,
Through the method of union that leads them in accord,
He gathered under his power and cleansed of their stench.
The emanations, a host of emanations of the being's wisdom,
Appeared as the host of mothers, Gaurī, Caurī and the rest,
And as lions, tigers and the like.

That Greedy Mistress of Desire,
Through the force of her lust, looked upon whom she saw
As, on the whole, her own husband,
But saw him blazing with a far greater splendor.
Through that thought a doubt arose:
"How wonderful! Compared with my own lord,
This one blazes with far more light and has greater splendor.
What kind of emanation is this?"
As she wavered in two minds,
Vajrakumāra, of supreme means,
Let out a rākṣasa cry three times,
And her doubting thoughts were cut off.
Under the power of her lust
She clung tightly to the buddha's body.
Just as, when a snake driven by heat
Coils about the trunk of a sandalwood tree,
The tree relieves the torment of heat without a thought,
So he, without attachment to anything,
Dispelled her torment completely.

Then that Ucchuṣma
Uttered this near-heart mantra,
The essence of the wish-fulfilling jewel
From which all arises as one desires,
Supreme in performing every action:
oṃ bhu ra kuṃ i ci ki phi u cchusma kro dha hūṃ phaṭ
Through this miracle that is not the real thing,
He gathered in the miracle of great Vairocana.
Bringing down the light rays of oṃ,
Which had been emanated first,
He placed them in the womb of the Greedy Mistress.
By the power of this manifest secret mantra,
That syllable too was transformed there
Into the very likeness of a rākṣasa,
And it was placed directly in her womb.
From the light rays of the vajra hook,
rakṣa śrī he ru ka
ru tra ma tra ha ha ha ma:
By the power of these syllables that generate, complete and destroy,
The blessing was made firm.
That being of awareness, not the real thing,
Was gathered into the body of the vajra father.
That violent and powerful being,
Through the miracle of the illusory vajra,
Having tamed those whom the buddhas find hard to tame,
Cast off that childish conduct,
The part of Māra and of the perverted world,
And with his mind upon the essence of awakening
Remained, definitively explaining the intention.
The emanated king Vajradhara
Was made Lord of Secrets, holder of the secret,
Of the great secret treasury, the sacred scripture
Renowned in the three abodes of the gods,
By the being who had formerly been regent.

Then that fierce preta
Came back to his own abode,
And his own abode, his enjoyments
And his retinue no longer appeared as before;
Things he had never seen before appeared almost everywhere.
He was utterly displeased
And thought, "Ah! What has become of this encampment of mine?"
At that time the attendant, the Mistress,
Turned to her own husband and said:
"Ah, my handsome hero!
Are you speaking words of evil suspicion
Here within the retinue?
Hero, what is your intention?
deṅ hun han phan ño pa ti
kho na am ca sma ra ca
My hero, my handsome one, is that so?
kun lan tra co pu thra tha
Where, what, how could it be mistaken?"
When she spoke these words,
Through lust for her radiance, and desire,
And thinking that she surely held his own lineage,
He took it as a means of delight.

Then from the Greedy Mistress,
As the sprout of a pu tra came forth,
A sound resounding three times rang out,
And by that fierce and glorious ferocious cry
Rudra, his consort and the others fainted.
When they came to and looked once more,
Upon the great Terrifier, the Blazing One,
With dark blue, ferocious matted locks...

With nine heads and eighteen arms,
His vajra wings spread over the sky;
His body hairs like red-hot iron hooks,
Spiders, scorpions, vermin and snakes,
In terrifying forms, spread over the ground.
A skull ladle, a stira, a noose of entrails,
Sun and moon, garuḍa, owl and hawk,
A vajra and a three-pointed spear,
A casket, a whisk and a drum;
Hurling Mount Meru round about the trichiliocosm,
Adorned and decked with a hundred thousand nāga kings,
Blazing like the fire at the end of the eon,
Frowning in wrath, with blazing eyes of fury,
Avalokiteśvara with twenty-seven eyes,
Treading down the eight gods with eight blazing feet,
Became the wrathful Vajra Rākṣasa,
Renowned above the gods and māras.

Then that preta Rudra,
His own mind's notions swelling,
With the savage cry of fierce pride
Roused the lords of his retinue:
"ru tra ma tra ma ra tra
Gather, gather, host of Maheśvaras!
Gather wholly, utterly, to your oath!
Deploy, deploy, host of Maheśvaras!
Deploy wholly, utterly, for the deed!
Close in, close in, host of Maheśvaras!
Close in wholly, utterly, upon this one!
Lords, here at my palace
Is this great malevolent one:
The time has come for you to destroy him.
If here you ponder what to do and what not to do,
You are on the very brink of destruction.
A friend who weighs what should and should not be done,
Better that there be none of those deliberations;
Since such a friend is but an illusion,
Who would call that one a friend?
A warrior who weighs what should and should not be done,
Better that there be none of those deliberations;
Since such a warrior is but an illusion,
Who would call that one a hero?
Pledges weighed as to keep them or not to keep them,
Better that there be none of those deliberations;
Since such a pledge is but an illusion,
Who would deem it sacred?
In all things, weighing what should and should not be done,
Better that there be none of those deliberations;
Since such conduct is but an illusion,
Who would call it doing?
For each one, here, the time has come for his deeds!"

When he had urged the Maheśvaras with these words, unable to bear the splendor of Māra, the armies of the sons of gods who belong to Māra's faction, tens of millions and hundreds of millions, together with the Maheśvaras, became utterly alarmed, agitated and ferocious, and went into a frenzy. Like a whirlwind of the poison of grief they gathered at Māra's palace and brandished Māra's standards. They massed clouds of weapons, spread the nets of Māra's magical illusion, raised their war cries, and charged.

Then the hosts of vajra horses, by the strength of their vajra stamping, with the fierce force of their swift and miraculous gallop, roused all the wrathful ones of the Great Vehicle with this galloping song of the horse that decides the race:

"hri With the blazing dance mudrās
That wholly transform the moods of the body,
Charming, heroic and terrifying,
Laughing, scornful and frightening,
Compassionate, fierce, peaceful and the rest,
Crush the lords!
hri Display a form more desirable than a thousand Madanas;
For the sake of taming, display a form more terrible than a thousand Yamas;
In peaceful form as well, show yourselves surpassing a thousand Brahmās;
Make all things appear as the three maṇḍalas!
hri Crush the arrogant ones to be tamed, crushing them through skillful means!
ho ho ha ha he he hi hi With joyous laughter,
Destroy all the lords, utterly destroy them!
By the power of supreme prowess crush the arrogant lord!
hri Great Mighty One, hu lu hu lu, draw forth from the Three-Eyed One the three accomplishments!
hri By the power of supreme prowess crush the arrogant Mighty One!
From the heart of Rudra Matra, Glorious Lady, draw forth, draw it fully forth!
By the power of supreme prowess crush the arrogant Brahmā!
From Brahmā draw forth Tārā, sma ra tha!
hri By the power of supreme prowess crush the arrogant Lord of Joy!
All you of great union, draw forth joy for me!
hri By the power of supreme prowess crush the arrogant Rudra!
Great union, draw forth the queen Krodhīśvarī, draw her fully forth!
Compassion that weighs what should and should not be done,
Better that there be none of those deliberations;
Since such compassion is but an illusion,
Who would call that compassion?
Those to be tamed, weighed as to whether to tame them or not,
Better that there be none of those deliberations;
Since such ones to be tamed are but an illusion,
Who would speak of taming them?
hri ha ha ho ho Emanate, emanate fully!
Emanate fully everywhere and make it appear!
Make the savage, fierce and furious tumult blaze!
Beings of many and various kinds who look with wrong views in wrath,
The many kinds of beings to whom wrath appears:
Skilled in taming the wrathful continua wholly, leaving none,
Supreme means of all the Victors, emanate illusion, fully emanate!
Unmoved by wrath, supreme of the four wisdoms, rise up everywhere and take hold!
Beings of many and various kinds to whom desire appears:
Skilled in taming wholly, leaving none, the continua of many kinds of attachment,
Supreme means of all the Victors, emanate as illusion, fully emanate!
Unattached to enjoyment, wisdom of supreme bliss, savor it everywhere, rise up and play!
Beings of many and various kinds to whom bewilderment appears:
Skilled in taming wholly, leaving none, the continua of many kinds of delusion,
Supreme means of all the Victors, emanate through illusion, fully emanate!
Unbewildered in sameness, wisdom unmingled and wholly perfect, rise up and contemplate!"

At these words, the fierce and joyful sound of the wrathful goddess, indignant and fearless, rang out like a thousand thunderclaps. The tens of millions and hundreds of millions of māras of Māra's faction drew in their four limbs and were overpowered. The massed clouds of weapons, the standards and the rest, cleared away into emptiness. Their harsh words became manifest as the sound of the Secret Mantra of the Great Vehicle, ringing out and resounding.

As for Black Rudra, lord of the charnel ground, his own retinue abandoned him entirely and went for refuge to that holy Lord. Bereft of every object of his intellect, dispirited, his intentions cut off and his means lost, wholly agitated and utterly confused, he brought forth the seeds of his own five Rudras, and said ru lu ru lu bhyo. He took on a wrathful form With three heads, six arms and four legs. That Lord, Vajra Rākṣasa, Did exactly the same there: Making the five seeds that bring under control, Blessed, indivisible letters, He snatched away the emanations of his speech, And Rudra's faculty of speech failed. Then that lord of the charnel ground Uttered ra la ra la bhyo, And became nine-headed and eighteen-armed. There that holy Lord Cut off the emanations of his speech for certain. Again that lord of the charnel ground Uttered hu lu hu lu bhyo, And became eight-headed and sixteen-armed. That greatly glorious Lord, the Rākṣasa, Did just the same there, And there too he cut off his utterance. Then that lord of the charnel ground Uttered ha la ha la bhyo, And with twenty-one heads Became forty-two-armed. Again Vajra Rākṣasa Did just that there, And cut off his faculty of speech. Once more he himself Uttered ra li ra li bhyo, And from his vast, immeasurable body Sent forth a whirlwind of the eon of disease And loosed malevolent weapons from his body. Lord Vajra Rākṣasa Did just the same to him: He snatched his speech, and the emanations of his speech were cut off. Like a man Whose tongue is cut and who is drenched in blood, He was utterly stupefied and fell down.

When that great Sovereign of all
Proclaimed sa ma ya hūṃ,
Through a connection from times of old
His life faculty could not bear it.
There, facing him,
With a blazing trident khaṭvāṅga,
Displaying the mudrā of the lord who destroyed the mountain of the putra,
He purified him outright.
Through the force of attendants, which linked him
To his own holy ones and the rest,
To buddhas without end
And to boundless minds of awakening,
And through the might of seeing Ghanavyūha,
The darkness of clinging from beginningless time
Cleared away, and finding births without end,
He remembered the deeds he had done in the bodies he had taken.
Brought out, he sat at the Lord's feet;
Bowing in great joy, yet as one in pain,
There he wept bitterly and cried out:
"Alas! Alas! Alas once more!
Hero, divine lord, your compassion is small!
From a single abode of perfect bliss
To drive me out, your compassion is small!
Now whatever you will do, do it quickly!
If you liberate me quickly, the bliss will be lasting.
I come as your subject and offer my body;
Do not be harsh with your subject!
From this day on, as you command,
I will carry out the word of your command.
If we do not act as commanded,
Or if we go against the holy word,
May our heads, bodies and hearts
Rot, decay, wither and burn!
Our mothers, sisters, daughters and wives
We offer as subjects, no small tribute.
We who lack the fortune to come to the center,
Place us at the edge of the blazing maṇḍala!
We who lack the fortune to partake of the first portion,
Grant us the leftovers, the rinsing water and what is spat out!"
To the grieving mothers and sisters of his attendants he said:
"From now on you are subjects of the Victor,
So whatever you do, be agents of the command;
Always swiftly accomplish what the command requires.
I too, having acted until now on the wrong path,
Have passed through the boundless sufferings of saṃsāra.
Now that compassion and my karmic remainder have met,
I go swiftly to the realm of Sukhāvatī.
Do not fail in the command taught to you after me!
As a loving mother longs for her child,
Long deeply for those who hold the pledges.
As a loving sister longs for her brother,
Cherish those with the branches tenderly.
Just as a girl longs for her companion,
Cherish the yogins dearly.
As a loving servant holds his master dear,
Keep close to the holy ones for certain."

Having given this advice to his retinue and attendants, he went for refuge to the Great Hero, and in order to guard the great command fully, he offered his own token, his life-essence, and swore an oath. With ru lu ru lu bhyo he staked his own life. Then the Blessed One gave his full consent, as follows: blessing him with oṃ ru lu ru lu bhyo ha, he burned, purified and tamed him, and remained at ease.

This was the twenty-seventh chapter, on the complete taming of Māra, from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

Then, at that time, the hosts of Maheśvaras thought: "How wonderful! This great Rudra, so mighty, so arrogant, so deluded, so lustful, so proud, so savage, has been overpowered by this Great Hero: definitively and truly overpowered, completely and utterly overpowered." So they thought.

At that time, Rudra, knowing with his own mind the thoughts in their minds, said: "O holy attendants, do not think like that! All you holy attendants bound to the remainder of my former karma, having met that spiritual friend, know karma. Birth is known. Karma is seen. Birth is seen. Karma has become manifest.

Birth has become manifest, and I have come to despair of myself on account of my own karma. In an instant I saw the svastika palace of great bliss. When women are tormented by the suffering of pregnancy, they think, 'This suffering comes solely from the karma of having gone to men and indulged in them often.' Just so, I too, utterly despairing over my karma alone, am in great anguish, for it bursts forth with the force of a great torment, like a landslide rolling down from a steep mountain peak that nothing can turn back. You attendants too, take your own karma to heart! The force of anguish will come rolling down like a landslide."

Having said this, he wept until he was exhausted and cried out bitterly, and gave voice to his complete anguish, as follows:

"Blessed One of great compassion, Vajrasattva,
Stainless, conch-colored, supremely beautiful in form,
Spreading the pure clear light of a hundred thousand suns and moons,
Hero blazing and shimmering with a thousand rays of light,
Renowned as the preceptor and teacher of the three worlds,
Sole kinsman of all beings of the three realms,
Loving protector, god of compassion, think of me!
From beginningless time until now
I have wandered the wheel of existence on wrong paths, having lost the path.
In former lives I erred, living a life of wrong deeds.
Whatever evil deeds I have done, I deeply regret their harm.
The force of karma, swollen and arrogant, grew so strong
That I have sunk into the ocean of saṃsāra's suffering.
The blazing fire of aversion burns my own being;
The thick darkness of delusion blinds my wisdom;
My consciousness sinks into the mire of the ocean of desire;
The mountain of fierce pride presses me down into the lower realms;
The whirling gale of jealousy sweeps me about in saṃsāra;
I am bound by the tight knot of the view of a self;
I have fallen into the trench of desire, like a pit of embers;
Fierce suffering pours down like unending rain.
Suffering such as this is very hard to bear,
And as the force of evil karma blazes like a fierce fire,
The sprouts of consciousness and the faculties are scorched.
When this illusory body of aggregates cannot bear it,
Can you, loving protector, compassionate one, bear it?
I am foolish and deluded, of bad karma, a great sinner;
Through the force of karma I was born in the desire realm as Rudra.
Born, I regret it; I despair of my karma.
Yet though I despair and regret, the force of karma cannot be undone.
The force of karma is like the course of a river;
How could the river of karma's power be turned back at once?
Every ripening arises from one's own karma.
Driven by that fierce gale of karma,
I, from then until now, for countless eons,
Have wandered in the dark prison of saṃsāra.
Through the blessing of your compassion, my attendant,
Karma, afflictions and obscurations have been wholly purified,
And now I sit at the feet of the one who loves me like a mother.
Your face of compassion, blazing with light like the sun and radiant like the moon,
Of which one never tires of gazing,
Since beginningless time I have not seen
With the bubble-like eyes blinded by the cataract of ignorance.
Where have you stayed until now, protector of beings?
Through the fierce and unbearable force of karma,
I am utterly afraid, my flesh creeps in terror,
And I utter this piteous cry of deep anguish.
Since I send forth the voice of the wretched and destitute,
If you, loving protector, do not consider me with compassion now,
Then when one day I die and pass on, parted from this body,
Parted from kin, friends and companions, Yama will lead me away.
At that time the worldly time will not stand empty:
Through the force of karma one like me will be led away alone.
At that time I will have no protector and no refuge,
So without putting it off until some later time,
Perform the fierce rite now, right away,
Upon a being like me, tormented by karma!
Because I have held wrong notions since beginningless time,
I have not been freed from the abode of saṃsāra's three realms.
For countless eons, in as many births,
I have taken countless bodies of coarse matter, which have perished.
If their flesh and bones were heaped up, they would equal the height of the world;
If their pus and blood were gathered, they would be the size of the great ocean;
If the remnants of their karma were gathered, they would be beyond thought and past telling.
Though I have counted through the three realms, birth and death succeeding one another,
The deeds I have done have all been wasted, to no purpose.
In all those countless births, however many,
If the deeds of even a single birth
Had been devoted wholly to unsurpassed awakening,
Then the deeds done that much would have had a purpose,
And in truth I would long since have passed into nirvāṇa.
Since the force of karma is strong and the power of the afflictions great,
I have taken bodies, nets of flesh and blood, and wandered in saṃsāra,
Falling into the prison of existence, its sufferings hard to bear.
Fierce, unending suffering such as this,
All of this wrongdoing, has arisen from my own karma.
With great compassion, cut the stream of my bad karma,
And turn back this wind of afflictions and karma, I pray.
Since the force of karma from ignorance and error is strong,
If I wander forever in the darkness of unknowing,
Will you not light my way with the rays of the lamp of wisdom?
If I cannot bear the fruit of my wrongful deeds,
Will you not perform the activity of great compassion?
When I have fallen over the precipice of distorted views,
Will you not seize me with the swift and rapid hand of compassion?
When I am tormented by the unbearable disease of the three poisons,
Will you not heal me with the medicine of compassion, skilled in means?
When the fire of suffering, the ripening of karma, blazes,
Will you not let fall a stream of cool rain of compassion?
When I sink into the mire of saṃsāra's suffering,
Will you not draw me out with the hook of compassion, skilled in means?
If, after being purified again and again in the abode of saṃsāra's three realms,
I should one day attain the fruit on my own,
What need would there be for the compassion of the noble ones? There would be no point.
If you leave me be because my karmic remainder is strong, consider:
What is called compassion, for whom would it do anything?
Hero, you possess the power of compassion,
And since the remainder of my karma, our past connection, is strong,
Without being careless, indifferent or idle,
Look upon me now from your heart, compassionate god, Victor!"

Saying this, he broke the mountain of his own pride, manifestly emanated his body as a lion, and offered it as a throne for the feet of the Lord.

This was the twenty-eighth chapter, on giving voice to the anguish of the lowliest, from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

Then, since that rākṣasa of karma had been refuted in his wrong views, in order to cut the stream of his karmic obscurations, Vajra Rākṣasa reviled him with the great eightfold reviling, as follows:

"This sinner, mighty in perversity,
Has surely wandered in the wheel of existence
From beginningless time until now.
That all of it arises from yourself,
The root of the wrongdoing, is surely the fault.
For as many countless eons,
Taking bodies in many births,
Countless heaps of form have perished;
Though their flesh and bones would fill the trichiliocosm
And their pus and blood would swirl into an ocean,
They were wasted to no purpose.
No one else did it: it is your own karma.
Had you spent even one portion of that for a purpose,
You would by now have attained buddhahood in truth.
The great mighty ones without attachment,
Who give away their bodies entirely,
Attain the undefiled bliss of liberation
Even by giving no more than flesh, bones and blood
To beings tormented by hunger;
What need to speak of seeing the profound meaning?
Not daring to give away your own body,
Clinging fixedly to the extreme of attachment,
You pass from one to another through saṃsāra's three realms,
Tormented by the disease of pain.
This falling into the prison of existence
Is surely the fault of tying knots in space.
This wandering in the expanse of darkness
Is surely the fault of delusion in particular.
This sinking into a mire without escape
Is surely the fault of craving and longing.
This torment by the heat of a wicked mind
Is surely the fault of heated deeds.
This being crushed by a mountain of suffering
Is surely the fault of piled-up pride.
This falling into the wind gate of saṃsāra
Is surely the fault of the wind of karma.
Rudra, you torment yourself:
If from the very beginning you are a child of the Victors,
Why do you circle in the wheel of existence?
It is therefore your own karma.
Mind itself is like the sky,
Obscured by the sudden clouds of appearing forms.
To purify the concepts of appearing forms,
Scatter them with the wind of the eon of wisdom.
First you erred by going against the command;
Then you piled up the mountain of the manifest;
Then you burned the wish-fulfilling jewel;
Finally, by engaging in the four acts,
The meaning of the yoga of the Great Vehicle
You did not realize, or realized wrongly,
Or realized partially, not realizing it truly.
Not practicing it as it is, through a mixed practice,
You bound yourself to the course of the paths of action of the lower realms,
And it ripened as the fruit of a perverse existence.
Alas, how pitiful is bad karma!
I am an agent of the command,
And since the time for the work of the command has come,
In order to draw out of the abode of saṃsāra
This one who wanders in the lower realms,
I shall cut the stream of bad karma.
This sin ripened into substance
I slay with the weapon of the vajra sky.
In the world of the eastern gateway of the path,
You who cut off the true path to liberation,
Savage bhūta, outcaste,
You obscure suchness.
With the intellect that is truly certain,
Your source cut and your root severed, you arose from self.
Through the distorted might and diligence
Of the concept of a self that clings to self,
You act as a caravan leader toward wretched states.
Since that very thing is the concept of a self,
I destroy it with the power of the sky of selflessness.
With the great means that dominates and subdues,
With the might that is manifest in cutting,
And with the blessing that torments,
I crush the lord of the obstructors of the three times.
a na ya mahā śūnyatā a ko ta ya nāṃ sarva pā paṃ ma taṅ kha ru tra naṃ sarva pā paṃ kha ṭvāṃ ka ru tra naṃ
Since all is emptiness and unborn,
In killing there is no one killed;
This miracle of purifying illusion
Is the pledge of great compassion.
This pledge of killing for certain
Is not killing that shows the meaning:
The consciousness, freed from form, is made a vajra.
To bring it back down is the supreme pledge:
Gathering the consciousness with hūṃ,
Bring that very same one down into its form,
And just as it abided before,
Purify it in sameness, as great bliss.
This vetāla vajra pledge
Is the heart pledge of all the Victors.
Whoever is slain with the vajra weapon,
His life, his wrong concepts, dies in the vajra.
Since there is never a time when he will rise,
How could there be any birth again?
If the consciousness is not separated from form,
It rises in liberation from the abode of saṃsāra.
The deathless vajra, the mind of awakening,
The nectar water of the wisdom of emptiness,
Heals all who have drunk great poison;
It is the supreme physician who raises all the dead."

When the Lord of Laṅkā had spoken thus, the being of Black Rudra was completely purified, and he saw for certain the higher meaning.

This was the twenty-ninth chapter, on the complete purification of his being, from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

Then, in order to turn definitively toward that very one, with the longing to lift the wheel of his feet upon the crown of his head, he roused him with this song, renowned for having been transmitted everywhere, called Urging the Intention of All Buddhas, saying:

"oṃ Since all dharmas are pure by nature,
I too am utterly pure by nature.
The self that is the supreme essence
Of the heart pledge of all the tathāgatas
Is applied to accomplishing the supreme
For all limitless sentient beings without exception;
So, through the gathering of the whole ocean of Victors,
Bring me forth as the essence of body!
Supreme jewel crown of wisdom,
Unsurpassed mass of splendor,
In whom all the jewels of the buddhas are gathered,
To the Utterly Victorious One I pay homage!

Supreme chief, wisdom of the essence,
Holder of the supreme and holy vajra,
Since you are the lord of the pledge,
Empower me as a sovereign king!
Since I am directly empowered by the vajra,
All the buddhas empower me this very day.
I am the supreme of all the Blessed Ones,
And to accomplish the supreme I shall take up the vajra.
Since you abide as the vajra king,
Vajra of body, speech and mind,
Through the supreme vajra pledge
Empower me with perfect accomplishment!
All dharmas have the defining mark of the vajra,
Yet the vajra too has no defining mark.
For the yogin who is like a vajra,
All things shine forth as the vajra itself.
This is the very melody of the buddhas' speech.
Holding the pledge of the wisdom being,
The sacred austerity of Vajrasattva
I shall uphold always.
hūṃ Guard the sacred pledge of Vajrasattva!
Bring the wisdom being near!
King of awareness, consider me steadfastly!
Lord of secrets, I pray you, nurture me!
Giver of supreme realization, bring forth delight in me!
You who cherish all desires, take delight in me!
Primordial buddha, compassionate in sameness,
Turn my mind to virtue in all deeds!
Sugatas of the three times, in all my abodes
Accomplish the vajra holder and the pledge being!
O tathāgata, delighted, delighted,
Vajra, never abandon me either!
O Vajra, lady of the whole dharmadhātu,
Great Lady of non-abiding passion,
Supreme source of everything without exception,
You are the most sacred of supreme bliss.
You are the chief of all pledges,
Mother who gives birth to Vajrasattva;
In the activity that accomplishes the aims of beings,
Bring union with compassion to perfection!
Since all the qualities of the buddhas arise from it,
The jewel vajra is unsurpassed.
Since I have sung the song of Vajradharma,
May I become one who performs the vajra of activity!"

With sa ma ya ho he took up the pledge. With sa ma ya stvaṃ he was fully bound by the pledge.

Then the son of Vajra Ucchuṣma, Who is no real entity, Vajra Rākṣasa, tamer of Māra, Gave him the sacred pledge of vajra force. With hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ bi śva vajra kro dha jva la ma ṇa ḍa la phaṭ phaṭ ha la ha la raṃ he purified him in the maṇḍala of fierce taming and purification. With ta keṃ ta keṃ phaṭ ha la ha la raṃ tīkṣṇa tīkṣṇa khaṃ kro dha kro dha yaṃ he purified all entities as empty. With oṃ he led him into the maṇḍala of spontaneously present nature. With muṃ he was placed in the maṇḍala of primordial suchness. With hūṃ he abode in the maṇḍala of the root, the mind of awakening. With i dan ti na ra gan te ba ri hṛ da ye nā ma basti nāṃ sa ma ya rakṣa si si ti pi na vajra a mṛ to ta kaṃ he gave him the sacred pledge. With oṃ āḥ hūṃ he gave him the sacred blessing of body, speech and mind. With oṃ vajra a mṛ ta e hye hi sarva ma na hūṃ hrī tha he gave him the sacred empowerment.

Then that rākṣasa of karma prayed to the deity of his own family:

"You who are my attendant,
Connected with me since lives of old,
Through the remainder of my karma and through compassion,
May you descend right now!"

When he had urged him in this way, glorious Vajrasattva, from the expanse of space, as a sun disc with the essence hūṃ together with its rays of light, dissolved into that one's heart, and so he was certainly established as of his own sacred family. With haṃ his vision was completely purified, and he saw all things as unmingled and wholly perfect. He was given the name Vajra and placed at the secret door of the Vajra Vehicle.

This was the thirtieth chapter, on Urging the Intention of All Buddhas, from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

Then, as for the great majority of the assembled host of attendants of that rākṣasa of karma: their own karma was purified, they saw many buddhas, and their beings were purified by the light rays of blessing, so that the stream of their karma and of karmic ripening, and their endless former and later births, passed away all at once. With their doubts cut off, Maheśvara and the other great gods wept bitterly and cried out, praying:

"Lord of Dance, listen and consider us!
We who cling to the extreme of Rudra
Have not loosened this net of saṃsāra, of body and of karma,
With the subtle reasoning of your command;
Not having opened it with the key of profound wisdom,
We lack the intelligence of self-aware wisdom;
Not having honored the counsel of the definitive good,
Not having been caught by the lightning noose of the means of dance,
Our consciousness is obscured amid the ocean of karma,
And we have not recognized that very nature as suchness.
We erred in grasping our own minds, in delusion, as a self.
Utterly remorseful, taking on the manner of the contrite,
We go for refuge to you, Great Hero.
To purify our substance, we offer our bodies as your seat;
With compassion skilled in means, please accept these bodies!"
Saying this, they offered their own bodies as a throne for his feet.

Then Black Throat and the other great nāgas wept and cried out, praying:
"King of splendor, sovereign, consider us!
We who wander in the net of existence
Are bound all round by the five bonds.
No one has bound us; there is no binder:
We are bound by the karma of concepts that grasp at a self.
In this prison of the three cities of self-grasping,
Our various bodies, our abodes of perception and the rest,
Consciousness is bound in the fetters of saṃsāra;
Since we have not loosened the knot of self-grasping,
The knots that bind us all round never cease.
From this suffering, indestructible and hard to untie,
Please untie the knot of compassion through skillful means!"
Saying this, they offered their bodies as ornaments for his body.

Then Shatrang Sengbu, king of the rākṣasas, and the others wept bitterly and cried out, praying:
"Great charming Hero, consider us!
We who wander in the land of sorrow
Are tormented and burned by a great blazing fire.
No one has burned us; there is no burner:
It arises from the fierce and inexhaustible ripening itself.
Aversion blazes and burns our own being,
And since we have not quenched the self-grasping blazing within,
The burning and torment all round never cease.
From this suffering, which cannot be turned back and is hard to quell,
Please let fall a stream of the cool rain of compassion!
Just as iron gathers to a lodestone,
From this day on we come as subjects of the Hero."
Saying this, each of them presented a skull cup filled with blood.

Then the host of those with perverse craving wept bitterly and, lamenting, made this request:
"O Lamp that illumines all and dispels the dark, please hear us!
We who wander in black and murky darkness
Have strayed from the way, lost the path, and are wholly confused.
No one has blocked us; there is no blocker:
We are veiled by the darkness of ignorance, deluded about a self.
Our consciousness is clouded over by the darkness of delusion,
And since, deluded about a self, we have not dispelled that darkness,
The black darkness that veils everything never ceases.
From this suffering of great murk, so hard to clear,
Please show us the bright lamp of supreme knowledge!"
Saying this, they went to him as their sublime refuge.

Then the host of humans and nonhumans also wept bitterly and, lamenting, made this request:
"O Physician of skillful means and compassion, please hear us!
We who wander in the three cities
Are in the grip of the chronic disease of saṃsāra.
No one has seized us; there is no seizer:
We are held by the disease of self-grasping, affliction and ignorance.
Our consciousness is tormented by unbearable piercing pain,
And since the chronic disease of grasping at things as real has not been cured,
The piercing pain that torments us all round never ceases.
From this suffering with no release, in which it is hard to live,
Please cure us with the medicine of skillful means and compassion!"
Saying this, they offered their bodies as his subjects.

Then the host of yakṣas wept bitterly and, lamenting, made this request:
"O Ship of skillful means and compassion, please hear us!
We who wander in the ocean of saṃsāra
Are sunk in the swirling depths of the waves of suffering.
No one has pressed us down; there is no presser:
We are pressed down by the mountain of thoughts that grasp at things as real.
We are swept about in saṃsāra by the river of karma,
And since the channel of grasping at things as real has not been diverted,
The fierce river of the force of karma never ceases.
From this suffering with no escape, so hard to stem,
Please carry us across in the boat of skillful means and compassion!"
Saying this, they went to him as their sublime refuge.

Then the host of bhūtas wept bitterly and, lamenting, made this request:
"O self-arisen King of Nectar, please hear us!
We who wander on the island of great poison
Are utterly intoxicated, drunk, and ruined.
No one has dosed us; there is no one who doses:
We are intoxicated by the poison of inverted views.
Our consciousness is tormented by the disease of great poison,
And since the root of the poison coiled within has not been cut,
The suffering of the blazing poison-disease never ceases.
Upon this inexhaustible suffering, so hard to bear,
Please anoint us with the pure, self-arisen nectar!"
Saying this, they went to him as their sublime refuge.

Then Vajra Rākṣasa spoke again:
"So it is! Excellent, children of good family!
It is the rudras that drive you round;
It is the rudras that crush you.
It is self-grasping that drives you round;
It is self-grasping that crushes you.
It is aversion that drives you round;
It is aversion that crushes you.
It is delusion that drives you round;
It is delusion that crushes you.
It is attachment that drives you round;
It is attachment that crushes you.
It is the four rivers that drive you round;
It is the four rivers that crush you.
It is wrong views that drive you round;
It is wrong views that crush you.
Since you are, in every respect,
Primordially children of the Victor,
Why do you circle in the three cities?
It is solely the karma of each one's own concepts.
Unless the waves of the ocean of karma,
The knot of self-grasping,
The great fire blazing within,
The black darkness that clouds everything,
The chronic disease of conceiving things as real,
The river of distraction,
And the roots of poison coiled within
Are dealt with by the self-arisen vajra,
Hoping in others, beseeching them and crying out in anguish
Is like chasing a mirage in want of water.
If you do not join yourselves to bliss right here,
How could another bring you to bliss?
While you abide in bliss primordially,
Where would you now go seeking bliss?
By seeking, you will never come to find it;
You only roam further into suffering.
Butter comes from milk,
Yet in that very milk there is primordially no butter;
Even the Victors, with their measureless supreme means,
Cannot obtain it except by churning.
Likewise, if even the Victors, seeking it in one's own suchness,
Do not find it there,
Would you look for bliss in something else?
How pitiful, you of small mind who do not understand!"

When he had spoken thus, Maheśvara, the Lord of Laṅkā, and the rest of the great assembled host said, "O great being, since we wish to see each our own suchness, how should we look at it?" To this request the great being replied, "O assembled host, children of the Victor, if you wish to see each your own suchness, you should enter solely through the gate of the three great maṇḍalas. And of these, you should enter solely through the gate of the root maṇḍala of the mind of awakening. Why is that? Because the three great maṇḍalas and each one's own suchness are not separate; they are not different. And that is because they are gathered in the root maṇḍala of the mind of awakening."

When he had said this, the great assembled host said, "Great Hero, please let us enter the three maṇḍalas. Great being, please let us enter them properly." When they had made this request, the great being said: "You children entering yoga Shall be drawn out of the six worlds And placed on the ground where the power of change is not yet fixed, At the border between the transcended and the not transcended." So he spoke.

Then the assembled host made this request:
"Great Hero, please hear us!
We, the fortunate assembled host,
Wish to enter this gate,
So please accept us as children of the Victor!"
When they asked this, the great being said:
"You great and fortunate assembled host,
Welcome! You have come well!
Whoever has come to this secret gate
Of the scripture of accomplishment will not turn back."
So he spoke.

Then those assembled generated the superior intention toward awakening, thus:
"Though we, the limitless sentient beings,
Are in truth buddhas,
Through the power of concepts we are in saṃsāra;
So we generate the mind toward supreme awakening.
Though the mind itself is buddha,
Veiled by darkness, we have not known it.
From this path of the mind of awakening
We shall enter the ground of buddhahood."
So they declared.

Then again the assembled host made this request:
"In this spontaneously present palace of great bliss,
From the four gates of liberation,
Open the gate of immeasurable compassion
And show us the fair face of loving-kindness!"
So they requested. Then the great being spoke these words:

"How wonderful, fortunate assembled host! To cut off every gateway to the paths of saṃsāra And to lead you onto the path of awakening, I shall open the secret vajra gate." When he had said this, light rays issued from the radiance of his smiling face, and the gate of certain entry into the meaning was opened.

Then the assembled host, delighted and pleased, earnestly aspiring to yoga, made this request:
"In the wondrous palace, the celestial mansion,
Where the whole ocean of Victors resides,
We are devoted with special certainty to yoga
And go for refuge to the vajra lineage."
So they requested.

Then again the assembled host made this request:
"From the beginningless limit of time,
Under the power of ignorance and wrong thoughts,
Whatever evil we have done,
All of it we confess, each one."
So they requested.

Again that assembled host made this request:
"Just as the Victor, the vidyādhara Vajra,
Liberated saṃsāra,
So, in order to liberate us completely,
Please bestow the vajra sun!"
So they requested.

Then the great being said:
"In what do you take joy?
In what do you have faith?
To what are you devoted?
What is it that you desire?"
When he had said this, the assembled host replied:
"We, the fortunate, in great bliss,
Take joy in the supremely joyful mind.
In the supreme meaning itself we have faith.
How wonderful! In this pleasure grove of supreme bliss,
Having practiced the deeds of a buddha so hard to do,
We are devoted to liberating all beings."
So they declared.

Again the assembled host made this request:
"Supreme Victor, great being, as you are,
Just so shall we practice.
Wondrous conduct of vast intent
We shall practice for the benefit of beings.
The worldly ones to saṃsāra's farthest reaches,
Those not yet delivered, we shall deliver;
Those not yet freed, we shall free;
Those not yet relieved, we shall relieve,
And in the realm of nirvāṇa
We shall join them to the very heart of awakening."
So they declared.

Then again the assembled host asked him to urge them to the sacred commitment (samaya):
"Indivisible yogin of the secret,
Arisen from the expanse of nondual meaning:
The expanse is the indestructible vajra,
And since it is one, without meeting or parting,
In the very expanse of the three secrets
Please keep us earnestly in nonduality."
So they requested.

Then the great being said:
"You yogins of the vajra holder,
Since you are certainly one in the vajra holder,
And since you are equal in the three secrets,
I give you the indivisible vajra commitment.
The indivisible vajra commitment
Is drawn out from the expanse of the three secrets.
The commitment of the three maṇḍalas,
Once accomplished, has no time of destruction."
So he declared.

Again the assembled host made this request:
"Dharma siblings, never meeting and parting,
Though of differing families, have a single commitment.
Since we are bound together so that it is hard to transgress,
Please keep us earnestly as holders of the commitment."
So they requested.

Then again the great being spoke these words:
"If you should deceive
The sublime ones of the three secrets,
They will all be enraged, and the great blood
They will draw from the violator's heart and drink."
Thus he gave the sublime commitment.

Then again that sublime being said:
"You of intelligence, buddha of the future,
In order to give birth to unsurpassed wisdom,
Abide in the three secrets that purify the mind stream.
Look into your own mirror itself.
Arranged in the three places,
The great secret, the secret, the supremely secret:
By seeing it you become suchness itself.
It is nothing other than self-arisen reality.
Do not speak of it to those of small mind!
If you should speak of it,
Broken by the fierce oath,
You will burn in the place of great blazing fire."
Thus he proclaimed the sublime commitment.

Lord of Laṅkā, when that assembled host had thus come to abide in the sublime three secrets, at that time some came to abide wholly in the yoga of aspiration. Some came to abide wholly in the yoga of opening the great family. Some came to abide wholly in the yoga of great reassurance. Some came to abide wholly in the yoga of receiving the great prophecy. Some came to abide wholly in the yoga of perfecting great power.

Thus at that very time, that great assembled host was empowered with the vajra name empowerment. Some became known as Vajra Sprout. Likewise some became known as Dharma Sprout. Some became known as Jewel Sprout. Some became known as Moon Power. Some became known as Sun Radiance. Some became known as Lion Power. Some became known as Sky Vajra. Among them, some of the wild ones became known as Vajra Wolf. Some became known as Vajra Juncture of Time. Some became known as Vajra Tamer of the Directions. Some became known as Vajra Charnel Ground. Black Rudra himself became known as Vajra Vetāla, Lord of the Charnel Ground. He remains for the duration of the great Good Eon, and becoming the protector of all yoga, the upāsaka called Black Excellent One, he performs the task of guardian for all who protect the Dharma. He received the prophecy: "In the eon after that, in the world system of the nadir called Covered with Ashes, he will become fully and perfectly awakened as the Tathāgata called Voice of the Lord of Ashes."

Lord of Laṅkā, at that time the world system shook in six ways. It shook, shook strongly, and shook strongly all over. It moved, moved strongly, and moved strongly all over. It rocked, rocked strongly, and rocked strongly all over. It roared, roared strongly, and roared strongly all over. It quivered, quivered strongly, and quivered strongly all over. When the edges rose, the center sank; when the center rose, the edges sank. When the east rose, the west sank; when the west rose, the east sank; when the north rose, the south sank; when the south rose, the north sank.

All the worldly ones too were amazed and offered praise. Some of the world with its gods rejoiced in the sublime secret of suchness. Some attained acceptance of unborn phenomena. For some, the Dharma eye became pure, dustless and stainless. Some came to abide on the ground of nonreturning. And some, every one of them, received prophecies.

At that time the Rudra Black Liberation and the other savage ones who bore human skulls prostrated to that great being, the Savage Vajra, in the manner of yoga, lowering the four points of the body to the ground. Joining their palms and bowing with the crowns of their heads, they said:

"a ho su kha kṣaṃ barya de ba śi śa. How wonderful, great being! A perfect quaking has occurred. Is it the blessing of the nature of reality? Perfect forms have appeared. Are forms purified in their own place? Are they forms of compassion? Perfect sounds have arisen. Are sounds purified in their own place? Are they sounds of wisdom? Perfect scents have arisen. Are scents purified in their own place? Are they the scent of the mind of awakening? Perfect tastes have arisen. Are tastes purified in their own place? Are they the taste of meditative absorption? Perfect touch has arisen. Is touch purified in its own place? Is it the touch of the sacred commitment? Perfect feelings have arisen. Are feelings purified in their own place? Is the feeling equanimity?

"We feel that our might and prowess have grown beyond what they were before. We feel that miraculous powers have arisen. We feel that we have gained mastery. We feel that awareness has dawned. Great being, you who have shown us this source of such things, this sublime secret of all the buddhas, so profound, so vast, supreme, sublime, splendid and excellent, you are our father. You are our mother. And not only you, great being: any person at all who abides in this sublime teaching is our father, is our mother. Even if existence itself were overturned, the command would not be overturned.

"O great being, what need is there to speak of you? Even a person who abides in this sublime teaching of yours we shall honor on the crowns of our heads, like the crest jewel of the lord of the gods. The spouses, sons, daughters, wealth, servants and servants' servants, retinues, relatives, kin by blood, descendants, and the people of the districts and lands of all who protect the Dharma, we too shall guard, shelter and hide. Whatever humans and nonhumans harm those who protect the Dharma, we shall utterly destroy and annihilate. We shall seal off boundaries and bind them. Whatever they intend and whatever they entrust to us, we shall fulfill it all completely by doing the work of servants.

"And they too, protecting the Dharma and abiding in accord with the Dharma, for the sake of a connection with us, are to keep the secret mantras of the great gods, which accomplish for us whatever we wish, the source of worldly manifest signs, which Vajra Great Might declared to be endowed with manifest power, together with the sacred substances, the auspicious tokens and the supports of signs. Why is that? Because these are our sacred bonds.

"O great being, it is like this: this secret mantra of ours, endowed with manifest power, has approach and accomplishment with four limbs. What are they? They are as follows. oṃ bhur khuṅ mahā pra ṇa ya bhurci bhurki byi ga ge bi ma na se e ho śi mo u cchuṣma kro dha hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svāhā. Great being, that is the limb of approach. oṃ bhu du gurma a pra ṇa bhurci ci kyi phyi man sye kha śo mo ga kro dha hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svāhā. Great being, that should be known as close approach. oṃ bhu du ku ru i ci ki phyi u cchusma kro dha hūṃ phaṭ. Great being, this should be known as accomplishment. oṃ bhurku ti phaṭ. Great being, this should be known as great accomplishment.

"Great being, if one completes a hundred thousand recitations of each of these, and only then engages in whatever activity is to be done, it will be accomplished without doubt. Why is that? Because the buddhas of the three times too have praised them as endowed with manifest power. Vajra Great Might too acted by means of this worldly manifest sign. We too act by means of this manifest sign of miracles. Great being, if there were no manifest signs for whoever earnestly pursues this secret mantra, no happiness would ever come, and the authoritative words too would surely be void. And we, for having fallen from the sacred pledge of our own truthful words, would without fail have our heads split and vomit blood.

"Therefore, great being, there is no doubt about this secret mantra. The sublime ones have taught it as a means to accomplish benefit for whomever it may be, to produce wonder, and to bring fools to trust the word of the Buddha. It is good if they always practice these four limbs."

When they had said this, that sublime being said: "a hoḥ! It is fitting. It is proper. It is so. It is certain. It is true. Excellent! You savage ones, together with your mothers, your wives, your sons and daughters, your servants and servants' servants: if you are not parted from love, you shall be made mothers. If you are not parted from ardent care, you shall be made sisters. If you are not parted from diligence, you shall be made servants. If you are not parted from the mind of awakening, you shall be made sons. If you are without meeting and parting, you shall be made disciples. If you are swift when urged, you shall be made messengers. If you act according to the command, you shall be bound by the command." So saying, he placed them in the ranks of great yoga and empowered them as guardians of the Dharma and of all who protect the Dharma.

This is the thirty-first chapter of the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas, the prophecy that Black Liberation, who had strayed, has not strayed.

From the account of how the Great Vehicle definitively arose, this is the sixth teaching on how it arose: the discussion of the wisdom of the Dharma seal, which tells the story of the complete taming of Rudra when the time had come for those to be tamed.

The fifth section begins.

Then again the Lord of Laṅkā, the bodhisattva, the great being, put a question to the Lord of Secrets about the seventh of the twelve phrases spoken earlier, "It also arose in appearing through blessing." He asked: "O Lord of Secrets, when you yourself, teaching how the sublime secret Great Vehicle arose, gave your explanation as a narrative, you said, 'It also arose in appearing through blessing.' How does that appear, and how is it known?"

He replied: "Lord of Laṅkā, it refers to suchness itself appearing fully to those persons who are to be tamed, wholly ripe as disciples now that the time for their taming has come.

The Lord of Laṅkā said, "Lord of Secrets, where and how do those holy beings appear, those who are to be tamed and are wholly ripe as disciples now that the time for their taming has come?"

When he had asked this, the Lord of Secrets said, "O Lord of Laṅkā, those holy beings who are wholly ripe as disciples come to appear in all things."

He asked, "How do they come to appear in all?"

He said, "Lord of Laṅkā, those holy beings are not other than suchness, the nature of reality of all phenomena, and from that they appear as all phenomena: as consciousness, wisdom, bodies, emanations, letters, words, teachings, bodily relics, sacred remains, and so forth. Lord of Laṅkā, they appear solely through the blessing of suchness. Lord of Laṅkā, just as the reflections of the sun, the moon and the hosts of stars in the expanse of the sky appear distinctly in clear water without moving from them, so all phenomena appear from the pure secret expanse, the nature of reality of phenomena. And all phenomena come down to just the five: body, speech, mind, qualities and activity. That is what appears as the gateway that illustrates the secret mantra of the Great Vehicle. Why is that? Because it is unerringly certain that the secret expanse, which is the nature of all phenomena, is itself the suchness of the secret mantra of the Great Vehicle. That is known, and has become known, as the essential body.

"Lord of Laṅkā, from the nature of reality of the mind there appear the four consciousnesses. The four consciousnesses are the root consciousness, the referential consciousness, the all-appearing consciousness and the engaging consciousness. From the four consciousnesses there appear the five great wisdoms. From suchness, the nature of reality of the mind, there appears the wisdom of the dharma expanse. From the root consciousness there appears mirrorlike wisdom. From the referential consciousness there appears the wisdom of equality. From the all-appearing consciousness there appears discerning wisdom. From the engaging consciousness there appears the wisdom that accomplishes activity. Lord of Laṅkā, this is not establishing as something what it is not, nor contriving what is unlike to be alike. Uncontrived, unadulterated, unmade and unfabricated, it simply appears so, by its nature, from the very beginning."

Then the Lord of Laṅkā asked, "Lord of Secrets, is this wisdom limited to just these five, or does it also appear as other, different forms?"

He said, "Lord of Laṅkā, depending on circumstances wisdom appears as five, and it is also inconceivable, sixteen instants, of a single family, threefold, the three suchnesses, the four yogas, and the one that is self-arisen. Lord of Laṅkā, from the five wisdoms, distinct and unmixed, there appear the five families, and from their complete perfection there appear the four bodies. That is to say: from mirrorlike wisdom, the root consciousness, there appear buddhas of the vajra lineage and class, all of them. From suchness, the wisdom of the dharma expanse, the nature of reality of the mind, there appear buddhas of the elephant lineage and class, all of them. That itself appears as what is called the essential body, and from the union of those two wisdoms there appears the dharma body. From the wisdom of equality, the referential consciousness, there appear buddhas of the jewel lineage and class, all of them. From discerning wisdom, the all-appearing consciousness, there appear buddhas of the lotus lineage and class, all of them. From the union of those two wisdoms there appears the body of perfect enjoyment. From the wisdom that accomplishes activity, the engaging consciousness, there appear buddhas of the youthful lineage and class, all of them. From that itself there appear the secret body and the various emanation bodies. From the various emanation bodies there appear various letters, various words, measureless gateways of Dharma, and various bodily relics and sacred remains. These are what appear as illustrations of the gateway of the Great Vehicle of secret mantra.

"Lord of Laṅkā, therefore secret mantra appears by its nature in all phenomena and in every way, and holy beings manifestly show it as appearing through the blessing of secret mantra. Lord of Laṅkā, why is that? Because there is no phenomenon at all that is not pervaded by secret mantra, that does not abide in it, or that has not become secret mantra. For some holy beings who have found the certainty of secret mantra unerringly, everything becomes a mirror for those who wish to see secret mantra. In every respect, from buddhas down to tiny crawling creatures, and from space down to atoms, everything makes it vivid, clear and distinct. It cuts through doubts. It becomes a sublime treasury. It becomes a great volume. It is an explanation on a vast scale. It proclaims so that all may understand. Lord of Laṅkā, therefore secret mantra simply appears by its nature, and holy beings simply show it through blessing."

The Lord of Laṅkā said, "Lord of Secrets, is the appearing of everything secret mantra? Or is the nonappearing of the nature of everything secret mantra? Or is something that is neither secret mantra?"

The Lord of Secrets said, "O Lord of Laṅkā, the appearing of everything is not secret mantra. If it were secret mantra, the very term 'secret mantra' would be contradicted, and the perceptions of sentient beings and the intention of the buddhas would be without difference. Nor is there secret mantra anywhere apart from that. If only what is other than that were secret mantra, then not everything would be included in the nature of reality. If the nonappearing of the nature of everything were secret mantra, then the nihilists, for their part, would have attained perfect completion. Nor is what is neither of the two secret mantra. If something that is neither of the two were secret mantra, then there would be no other place where something called secret mantra could be found by seeking.

The Lord of Laṅkā said, "Lord of Secrets, then what is it that is called secret mantra?"

The Lord of Secrets said, "Lord of Laṅkā, what is called secret mantra is that suchness which is the nonduality of appearing and not appearing, which is free from the essence of the two, and which is not apprehended even as a middle."

The Lord of Laṅkā said, "Lord of Secrets, then if the essence of secret mantra is so very hard to fathom and hard to see, by what should it be seen?"

The Lord of Secrets said, "Lord of Laṅkā, the essence of secret mantra should be seen by a mind that is extremely subtle, most subtle, extremely profound, most profound."

He said, "Lord of Secrets, what is a subtle mind, an extremely subtle, a most subtle one? And what is an extremely profound, a most profound one?"

He said, "Lord of Laṅkā, as for an extremely subtle, most subtle mind, it is like this. There are some who say: 'In this element of earth, in a single league there are one akṣobhya of niyutas, thirty hundred thousand koṭis of niyutas, a hundred times sixty koṭis, thirty-two koṭis, five hundred thousand and twelve thousand of the most subtle atoms.' And they then divide these further into the most subtle atoms of fire, water and wind, and calculate by reckoning how many of the most subtle atoms there are in one great trichiliocosm, a great world system. Again, examining the endless different shapes in this great outer earth, they analyze each one by virtue of the differences, saying, 'These elements are flickering, warped, slanting, bent, entwined, coiled, jutting, ranged in rows, crooked, drooping, bulging, clinging, contorted,' and so on. And again, following the various names, conventions, signs, designations and imputations, and following their endless individual characteristics, they say that these people of many kinds of thinking and far-reaching analysis are, by these very means, on their way to the causes of omniscience. Lord of Laṅkā, such people are not called extremely subtle and most subtle; they are called coarse and most coarse. Why is that? Like fools who, wishing to sift gold dust from sand, mistake glittering mica for gold dust and search for it, they will not see or find all aspects. Why is that? Because in that there are all the aspects, but there is no wisdom.

"Lord of Laṅkā, a subtle, most subtle mind is like this: by knowing unerringly the suchness that is the nature of reality of a single phenomenon, one knows unerringly the suchness that is the nature of reality of all phenomena. The most subtle atoms, the various shapes, the names, conventions, signs, designations and imputations, all come to be known naturally. For in that, all aspects and wisdom are nondual and free from the essence of the two, and so there is not a single phenomenon that is not seen and not known.

"Lord of Laṅkā, what is a profound, most profound mind? There are some who seek and analyze the limits, depths, extremes and center of all phenomena. They are not called profound and most profound of mind; they are called those who have reached a dead end. Why is that? Like the deluded who mistake a mirage for water and chase it, or the blind who seek the edge of space, though they seek the limits, depths, extremes and center of all phenomena, they will not find them, and there is nothing to analyze either. Lord of Laṅkā, however, understanding that all phenomena are without limit and depth, without extreme and center: that is called a profound, most profound mind. Why is that? Because the whole intention of all the buddhas is free of conceptual examination and analysis. Lord of Laṅkā, therefore, since the essence of secret mantra is so very hard to fathom, it should be seen by a subtle, most subtle, profound, most profound mind."

He said, "Lord of Secrets, by relying on what example should that secret expanse be understood?"

He said, "Lord of Laṅkā, there is nothing in the world that can serve as an example for that secret expanse. For the time being, if one wants a directly perceptible example, one may take space. Not that space is comparable to it either; but since there is no directly perceptible example in the world beyond that, the sublime ones have explained that one should rely on space as an example and understand it from that."

He said, "Lord of Secrets, how should the secret expanse and space be matched in their qualities?"

He said, "Lord of Laṅkā, the secret expanse and space are not alike in every quality, because the one is self-aware emptiness and the other is emptiness without awareness."

He said, "Lord of Secrets, then is space something not included in that secret expanse?"

He said, "Lord of Laṅkā, what do you think? Space is included in the secret expanse, but the secret expanse is not included within space."

He said, "Lord of Secrets, what is space?"

He said, "Lord of Laṅkā, space is simply whatever gives room."

He said, "Lord of Secrets, then how is the secret expanse illustrated and understood by means of space?"

He said, "Lord of Laṅkā, it is taken as an example of similar qualities: whether there is room or not, it knows no exhaustion, and in emptiness everything arises. In part these qualities are similar, but as an example of self-awareness it has no part. Since that secret expanse is self-aware of everything, it is called self-arisen wisdom. In some contexts that wisdom is called nonconceptual wisdom. In some it is called the wisdom of emptiness. In some it is called the wisdom of the dharma expanse. In some it is called pure, luminous wisdom. In some it is called the wisdom of equality. In some it is called discerning wisdom. In some it is called the wisdom that accomplishes activity. Lord of Laṅkā, these are also called spacelike wisdom, mirrorlike wisdom, wisdom like the vast, wisdom of various arrays, and wish-fulfilling-jewel wisdom.

Lord of Laṅkā, the mirror is taken up as an example that shares certain qualities: it removes faults, it brings forth what is good, it makes things clear, reflections arise in it, it is effortless, it is free of deliberate formation, it is not conjoined, it is not unconjoined, it is not near, it is not far, everything arises in it, its measure is not delimited, it is not other, it is not obscured, it is not dark, and it is not remote. Lord of Laṅkā, although those qualities correspond in part, as an example of self-awareness itself there is no such part.

Lord of Laṅkā, the vast is taken up as an example that shares certain qualities: it is awareness itself, complete. It is complete without great or small, without long or short, without good or bad, without faults or virtues, without accepting or rejecting, without right or wrong, without pure or impure, without happiness or suffering, without high or low, without wide or narrow, without superimposition or denial, without mixing, and complete without division. Lord of Laṅkā, although those qualities correspond in part, as an example of self-awareness itself there is no such part.

Lord of Laṅkā, the array of variety is taken up as an example that shares certain qualities: abiding, engaging, being unmixed, clarity, distinctness, various enjoyments, appearance, and being utterly free of confusion. Lord of Laṅkā, although those qualities correspond in part, as an example of self-awareness itself there is no such part.

Lord of Laṅkā, the precious jewel is taken up as an example that shares certain qualities: it is nonconceptual, it performs activity, what is desired arises from it, it fulfills needs, it satisfies hopes, it is unceasing, it produces joy, it is inexhaustible, it establishes beings in happiness, it is accomplished in every way, it is unobstructed, it needs no support, it gives bountifully, and it accomplishes every purpose. Lord of Laṅkā, although those qualities correspond in part, as an example of self-awareness itself there is no such part.

He said, "Lord of Secrets, this word wisdom, by force of what meaning is it a designation?"

He replied, "Lord of Laṅkā, because it is so named from knowing all phenomena, in the unerring meaning of how they primordially are, as without arising or ceasing, it should be understood as a designation by force of that very meaning.

"Lord of Laṅkā, furthermore: being like space, being like a mirror, being like a rainbow, being like shape and color, being like clouds and mist, and appearing unfabricated; although those qualities correspond in part, as an example of self-awareness itself there is no such part.

"Lord of Laṅkā, illuminating everything, unobstructed gateways, the emergence of miraculous displays, gathering into one, and perfection: these should be understood as the performing of activity. Lord of Laṅkā, being unmixed, being complete, the totality of unmixed completeness, continuity, and nonreferentiality: these should be understood as what appears to be seen. Lord of Laṅkā, being unceasing, not projected outward, not sought, without remainder, and having become the cause: these should be understood as the potency of its dynamic power.

"Lord of Laṅkā, thus, from mind there is the appearance of mind, and appearance is to be recognized as wisdom. These too should be understood as the appearance of the blessing of the sacred secret suchness."

When he had said this, the doubts born of conceptual thought in the minds of the Lord of Laṅkā and the other beings in lowly bodies were cut off.

This was the thirty-second chapter, Arraying the Sacred Ornament of the Appearance of Enlightened Mind and Wisdom, from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

Then again the Lord of Laṅkā, the bodhisattva, the great being, asked the Lord of Secrets, "Lord of Secrets, what is the suchness of mind? What is consciousness? What is wisdom? And what is called the body?"

The Lord of Secrets said, "Lord of Laṅkā, as to what you have asked, I have heard it thus. Mind is mind, the appearance of mind, and what arises from mind: that is, it itself, its engagement, and its continuity. Consciousness is the all-ground, the mentation that sets it in motion, and the five doors that arise in continuity. Wisdom is whatever is recognized as that itself. The body is the ground on which arising and engaging appear."

Again the Lord of Laṅkā asked, "Lord of Secrets, what is recognition? What is arising? What is engaging? What is appearance? What is the ground?"

The Lord of Secrets said, "Lord of Laṅkā, as to the five you ask about, I have heard it thus. Recognition is having grasped it without its having come from anywhere else. Why is that? Lord of Laṅkā, if it were something that came from elsewhere, it would be adventitious, and so it could not properly be what is called wisdom. Arising refers to the emanation. Why is that? The emanation is what arises in continuity wherever it has never arisen or been experienced before. Engaging is the perfect enjoyment. Why is that? It is that by which yoga engages what genuinely abides within. Appearance is the dharma body. Why is that? It appears as the nature of two arising from the union of two. The ground is the body of essential nature. Why is that? Because it is the essential nature without substance that is the ground from which all phenomena arise."

Again the Lord of Laṅkā asked, "Lord of Secrets, what is the emanation? What is the perfect enjoyment? What is the dharma body? What is the essential nature?"

The Lord of Secrets said, "Lord of Laṅkā, as to these four that you ask about, I have heard it thus. The emanation is what arises in continuity out of nonexistence. The perfect enjoyment is perfect maturation. The dharma body is complete purity. The essential nature is suchness just as it is."

Again the Lord of Laṅkā asked, "Lord of Secrets, what is arising in continuity? What is perfect maturation? What is complete purity? What is suchness just as it is?"

The Lord of Secrets said, "Lord of Laṅkā, the four you ask about are these. Arising in continuity is the unbroken continuity of what has transformed from mind. Perfect maturation is what arises from mind. Complete purity is the appearance of mind. Suchness just as it is is mind itself."

"Lord of Laṅkā, why is that? When the darkness of what has transformed is cleared, the emanation manifestly appears. When the darkness of what arises is cleared, the perfect enjoyment manifestly appears. When the darkness of what appears is cleared, the dharma body manifestly appears. As for the essential nature, because it has passed beyond darkness, appearance, clearing, and obscuring, it is entirely free of the pathway of words. Lord of Laṅkā, it should be understood that within these themselves there is purity and impurity, by virtue of the distinctions of the secret essence."

He said, "Lord of Secrets, what is purity? What is impurity?"

He replied, "Lord of Laṅkā, it is like this: localized purity, partial purity, complete purity, and the great purity that is pure in every way and in every respect.

"Lord of Laṅkā, among these, localized purity is this: the pacification of whatever appears as three is utter purity; the lord of Dharma is maturation; the regent of Dharma is continuity.

"Partial purity is this: self-awareness within what is unaware is utter purity; having entered into bliss is maturation; whatever appears is continuity.

"Complete purity is this: space itself is utter purity; enjoying bliss is maturation; complete transformation is continuity.

"The great purity that is pure in every way and in every respect is this: what appears as the ground, what is unadulterated, is suchness itself; the absence of a body is utter purity; complete perfection is maturation; arising as anything at all is continuity.

"Lord of Laṅkā, among these, localized purity, partial purity, and complete purity are called impure. Why is that? Because they hold that there is purity in what, in every way, has nothing to be purified, and they set about purifying it."

He said, "Lord of Secrets, then why are localized purity, partial purity, and complete purity called pure?"
