He replied, "Lord of Laṅkā, it is because, from what was entirely obscured, they are cleared locally, cleared partially, and cleared completely. Lord of Laṅkā, what in every way and in every respect has nothing to be purified is what is called pure. Why is that? Because in it there is no cause whatsoever for purification."

Again the Lord of Laṅkā asked, "Lord of Secrets, what is continuity but not maturation? What is maturation but not continuity? What is both? What is neither?"

To this question he replied, "Lord of Laṅkā, the four questions you have asked are answered thus. Continuity but not maturation is one who, although passed beyond, benefits immeasurable saṃsāra through the power of the lotus. Maturation but not continuity is the stage prior to the grounds. Both is having passed beyond with remainder. Neither is utter purity."

Again the Lord of Laṅkā asked, "Lord of Secrets, what is their activity? What is their body? What is their intention? What is their ground?"

He replied, "Lord of Laṅkā, their activity is the activity of all buddhas: the action of continuity, appearing as anything at all. Their body is the body of all buddhas: complete maturation. Their intention is the intention of all buddhas: utter purity. Their ground is the source of all buddhas: suchness itself.

"Lord of Laṅkā, these are also called many, one, one and many, and beyond these. Why is that? Continuity is called many, because its variety is beyond measure. Maturation is called one, because it is one in all. Utter purity is called one and many, because it is the ground of continuity and maturation. Suchness is called beyond these, because it neither diminishes nor increases through their arising and entering."

Again the Lord of Laṅkā asked, "Lord of Secrets, are these permanent, or are they impermanent? Are they existent, or are they nonexistent? Are they one, or are they different?"

To this question the Lord of Secrets replied, "Lord of Laṅkā, the six questions you have asked are answered thus. They are permanent. They are not impermanent. Nor are they permanent as permanent and impermanent as impermanent. Nor are they neither permanent nor impermanent. They are existent. They are also nonexistent. They are also existent as existent and nonexistent as nonexistent. They are also neither existent nor nonexistent. They are one. They are also different. They are also neither one nor different. They are also free from the essential nature of one and different.

"Lord of Laṅkā, why is that? It is like this. Because they are unchanging and without any antidote, because whatever arises is not apprehended, because they are the ground of all, because no extreme whatsoever is apprehended, because they are not everlasting, because they arise in continuity, because they are the sacred intention, because they are without substance, because they are the domain of a purified continuum, because they have passed beyond extremes and middle, because they have passed beyond conventions, because they are just as they primordially are, because they are unthinkable, because they are inconceivable, because they are the domain of each one's own experience, and because they are alike in every particular: take them as permanent, take them as impermanent, take them as existent, take them as nonexistent, take them as one, take them as different, and take them as free from the essential nature of one and different.

"Lord of Laṅkā, thus, because of the needs of those to be trained, the bodies have no fixed number: take them as inconceivable. Take them as five, take them as four, take them as three, take them as two, take them as one. In truth, they are merely designated by convention as body, speech, mind, qualities, and activity; but the body is space, speech is echo, mind is the nature of reality, qualities are illusion, and activity is emanation. These merely appear through blessing according to whoever is to be trained; in reality there is no appearance whatsoever.

"Lord of Laṅkā, their essence is consciousness; recognized, it is wisdom; made directly manifest, it is buddha. Lord of Laṅkā, thus, because the nature of secret mantra is so hard to fathom, those of the realm of darkness designate it consciousness, those of the realm of light designate it wisdom, and those whose eyes are clear designate it buddha.

And those with sacred eyes recognize it as secret mantra."

Then again the Lord of Laṅkā asked, "Lord of Secrets, what are their buddhafields? What is their retinue? What teaching do they proclaim? What is their time? What is their activity?"

The Lord of Secrets said, "Lord of Laṅkā, the five questions you have asked are answered thus.

"For what is without essential nature, the buddhafield is unproduced, the retinue is without any awakened one, the teaching is unthinkable, the time is beyond measure, and the activity is acting as anything at all.

"For utter purity, the buddhafield is the great all-pervading expanse, the retinue is an ocean-like assembly of wisdom, the teaching is the Dharma of nonaction, the time is in every way and in every respect, and the activity is illuminating all phenomena.

"For maturation, the buddhafield is Sukhāvatī, the retinue is an ocean-like assembly of the completely matured, the teaching is inexpressible, the time is whenever one dwells in the Pure Abodes, and the activity is sacred yoga itself.

"For the secret, the buddhafield is the Realm Possessing Lotuses, the retinue is an ocean-like assembly of victors, the teaching is the very ocean of secret mantra, the time is the very time when, as the Uṣṇīṣa Cakravartin wishes, the bodies of all the victors are assembled, and the activity is cutting the doubts of sacred beings concerning the sacred intention.

"For continuity, the buddhafield is the Realm Possessing Devotion, the retinue is an assembly of differing inclinations, the teaching is inconceivable, the time is when lamps are fully arrayed, and the activity is dispelling the darkness of whoever it may be.

"Lord of Laṅkā, these too should not be viewed as separate. Why is that? They merely appear in this way out of what is without essential nature; that is solely the appearance of the blessing of suchness, the sacred secret. Lord of Laṅkā, therefore, the sacred secret that is without essential nature is like the orb of the sun, and what appears from its blessing should be understood to be like its rays and light."

When he had said this, the doubts of the Lord of Laṅkā and the other beings in lowly bodies, which arose from conceptualizing the bodies, were cut off.

This was the thirty-third chapter, Arraying the Sacred Ornament of the Complete Appearance of the Bodies, from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

Then again the Lord of Laṅkā, the bodhisattva, the great being, in order to gauge how the blessing of the Tathāgata's speech appears, put a question: "O Lord of Secrets, those whose appearances are arrayed from the blessing of mind and body: how should one gauge what appears from the blessing of their speech?"

When he had asked this, the Lord of Secrets said, "Lord of Laṅkā, the question you have asked is answered thus. In the secret, the sacred secret without essential nature, there is no convention of words and sounds; in every way and in every respect there is simply no speech at all. Should one try to gauge those secrets, even a sacred being who is completely pure cannot gauge them; for at the very moment one gauges it, it is ascertained to be the sacred secret without essential nature, and so it is extremely hard to gauge.

"Lord of Laṅkā, nevertheless, it should be understood in this way. The speech of what is without essential nature is like space. The speech of utter purity is like the orb of the sun. The speech of complete maturation is like a mirror. The speech of the sacred secret is like a lion. The speech that arises in continuity is called inconceivable, because from a single utterance branches arise beyond conception.

"Lord of Laṅkā, these inconceivable ones arise as four and appear as four, and they should be understood in detail from those four. What are the four? They should be understood as the nature of speech, its modes, its branches, and its reflections.

"Lord of Laṅkā, among these, what is its nature? It is like this: it is great in resonance, it is all-pervading, it is quickly understood, it cuts doubts, each one thinks it is looking at him alone, it teaches each in due measure, it enters everywhere, it arouses aspiration, it engages each in particular, and it tames all.

"Lord of Laṅkā, what are its modes? It is like this: like Brahmā, like cymbals, like song and dance, like the kalaviṅka bird, like the roar of thunder, and like an echo.

"Lord of Laṅkā, its branches are these: it generates understanding, it is intelligible, it is worth hearing, it is without discord, it is extremely profound, it is consistent, it cannot be overpowered by anyone, it is pleasing to the ear, it is untroubled, and it is extremely clear. In each and every gateway of the modes, wherever it arises, all the branches arise complete.

"Lord of Laṅkā, among these, what are its reflections? It should be understood that the reflections appear as collections of letters, and that the letters arise from the blessing of the sacred secret. Lord of Laṅkā, the blessing of the sacred secret is not confined to letters: all phenomena, letters and the rest, arise out of nonexistence and take shape just as they are designated, are accomplished just as they are practiced, and appear just as they are looked upon wherever they are seen. Therefore letters too merely arise as the reflections of speech from the blessing of the unobservable expanse.

"Lord of Laṅkā, and yet, since everything is empty by its intrinsic nature, naturally unfabricated, and luminous, there is nothing to be sought; since it is primordially awakened, to pursue and strive for awakening is to undertake needless hardship."

He said, "Lord of Secrets, then why are sentient beings and buddhas two and different?"

He replied, "Lord of Laṅkā, that is because of conditions just as they are. Lord of Laṅkā, therefore it is like this: the cause of reflections, the gateway from which reflections arise, the dhāraṇī of reflections, the pledge of reflections, and the emanation of reflections.

"Lord of Laṅkā, among these, the cause of reflections is clarity that is not apprehended: from nothing at all, anything at all arises. Lord of Laṅkā, the gateway from which reflections arise is wherever two come together: from what is nothing at all, it brings awareness of anything at all.

"Lord of Laṅkā, although reflections indicate something, the actual basis neither arises from the reflections nor fails to arise, neither appears nor fails to appear, neither pervades nor fails to pervade. For example, everyone can utter the word 'space,' but if one asks what space is, no one at all can point it out, saying, 'It is just this.' Lord of Laṅkā, in the same way, reflections can indicate the actual basis, but they cannot point it out, saying, 'It is just this.' Why is that? Because reflections arise from the actual basis, they can indicate it; and because they are reflections, they become anyone's domain of experience. But the actual basis has become no one's domain of experience, and so it cannot be pointed out, saying, 'It is this.'

"Therefore, that secret intention of all buddhas, whether buddhas appear in the world or do not appear, whether sentient beings awaken or do not awaken, abides primordially as the nature of all and pervades all, yet it is not seen by all. Everything that arises from it may be known, and it may indicate that very thing; yet what arises from it cannot point out that very thing, saying, 'It is just this.'

"Lord of Laṅkā, the dhāraṇī of reflections is this: that very thing which is nothing at all and unseen arises as anything at all and is never separate from anything whatsoever. It is like this: whatever is a reflection is the actual basis. Whatever is the actual basis is the reflection. Whatever is the actual basis and the reflection is the expanse of the sacred secret. Whatever is the sacred secret is the actual basis and the reflection.

"Moreover, the sacred secret is like the ocean, and all that arises from its blessing is like the waves. To see these two as nondual, freed from the extremes of two, not apprehended even as a middle, never becoming two: that suchness is the dhāraṇī. Likewise there are the dhāraṇīs of being indivisible and indestructible by anything, of being apprehended nowhere, of equality, of changelessness, of the nature of space, and of name, word, meaning, and secret mantra.

"Moreover, the nature of reflections is the unobservable expanse, and the nature of the actual basis is likewise nothing but the unobservable expanse; therefore that is the dhāraṇī of both. That unobservable expanse everywhere gives rise to all, sentient beings, buddhas, and the rest; and since it is their nature, they are one. Hence it is the dhāraṇī of nonseparateness.

"Lord of Laṅkā, the pledge of reflections is this: a reflection does not depart from that of which it is the reflection. However it may appear, it does not change from or depart from that actual basis. Thus, since there is no arising or entering, no outer or inner, no front or depth, no meeting or parting, it is the pledge. Because all things never depart from that state, it is called the pledge.

"Lord of Laṅkā, for example, Vajrasattva is the sacred intention, the domain of experience of the buddhas. If a reflection of him were set before some fool, what would that fool think?"

He said, "Lord of Secrets, he would think, 'This is not Vajrasattva himself; it is merely his likeness.'"

He said, "Lord of Laṅkā, therefore, when the sacred ones look, in the unobservable expanse Vajrasattva and his reflection do not abide separately as one thing and another. Why is that? Because the nature of both is suchness, just as it is. If he were something separate, it would not be right for a result to arise from focusing on him."

The Lord of Laṅkā said, "Lord of Secrets, is there no difference between them?"

He said, "Lord of Laṅkā, under the sway of unknowing they are merely spoken of, and merely appear, as a reflection and the meaning it indicates. But when the sacred ones apply themselves to suchness, there is not the slightest difference between them."

He said, "Lord of Secrets, then what purpose do expression, indication, and convention serve for phenomena?"

He said, "Lord of Laṅkā, I have already dispelled that doubt."

He said, "Lord of Secrets, then what is the purpose of those of great mind striving with great hardship and fashioning images well?"

He said, "Lord of Laṅkā, that is so; yet it is in order to guide those attached to signs, and because those who, though of great mind, have not yet found the experience of attaining a great aim through a mighty means of little effort.

"Lord of Laṅkā, from suchness miraculous displays continuously appear. Like a jewel or a cloud, anything at all appears from nothing at all, and much spreads forth from little; therefore this appearance of all the phenomena of existence and nirvāṇa appears from nothing at all. Therefore these reflections of speech too should be understood as the nature of reality appearing from nothing at all.

"Lord of Laṅkā, the emanations of reflections are distinct. The reflection of speech is what appears as letters, and the emanations of letters are these: their names, their modes of origin, their forms, their natures, their sounds, their functions, their gateways, their synonyms, and their bases. One will see and know the suchness of these letters, the sacred secret, and through that one will become skilled in all phenomena and be liberated.

"Here, Lord of Laṅkā, the names of the letters are these: designations that indicate something, though it exists in nothing. The letter that indicates nonarising is designated by the name a. The letter that indicates action is designated by the name ka. The letter that indicates pervasion is designated by the name kha. The letter that indicates birth is designated by the name ga. The letter that indicates liberation is designated by the name gha. The letter that indicates grasping is designated by the name ṅa. The letter that indicates conduct is designated by the name ca. The letter that indicates meeting and parting is designated by the name cha. The letter that indicates nonabiding is designated by the name ja. The letter that indicates stain is designated by the name jha. The letter that indicates knowing is designated by the name ña. The letter that indicates suchness is designated by the name ta.

"The letter that indicates the agent is designated by the name tha. The letter that indicates pacification is designated by the name da. The letter that indicates phenomena is designated by the name dha. The letter that indicates name is designated by the name na. The letter that indicates the path is designated by the name ṭa. The letter that indicates abode is designated by the name ṭha. The letter that indicates total delusion is designated by the name ḍa. The letter that indicates intrinsic nature is designated by the name ḍha. The letter that indicates the object is designated by the name ṇa. The letter that indicates the ultimate is designated by the name pa. The letter that indicates the result is designated by the name pha. The letter that indicates bondage and liberation is designated by the name ba. The letter that indicates existence is designated by the name bha. The letter that indicates mindfulness is designated by the name ma. The letter that indicates the changeless is designated by the name ya. The letter that indicates causes and conditions is designated by the name va. The letter that indicates dust is designated by the name ra. The letter that indicates the vanquished is designated by the name la. The letter that indicates peace is designated by the name śa. The letter that indicates the generating of knowledge is designated by the name ṣa. The letter that indicates knowledge of all aspects is designated by the name sa. The letter that indicates cause is designated by the name ha. The letter that indicates final exhaustion is designated by the name kṣa.

"The letter that indicates self is designated by the name a. The letter that indicates distraction is designated by the name i. The letter that indicates a mountain is designated by the name ī. The letter that indicates attachment is designated by the name u. The letter that indicates darkness is designated by the name ū. The letter that indicates torment is designated by the name e. The letter that indicates malice is designated by the name ai. The letter that indicates destruction is designated by the name o. The letter that indicates emptiness is designated by the name au. The letter that indicates setting is designated by the name a. The letter that indicates the body is designated by the name aṃ. The letter that indicates speech is designated by the name āḥ. The letter that indicates the mind is designated by the name hūṃ. The letter that indicates qualities is designated by the name sva. The letter that indicates activity is designated by the name hā.

"The letter that indicates summoning is designated by the name Hook. The letter that indicates gathering is designated by the name Boundless Light. The letter that indicates leading is designated by the name Hope. The letter that indicates joining is designated by the name Crescent Moon. The letter that indicates name is designated by the name Drop. The letter that indicates cutting is designated by the name Staff.

"Lord of Laṅkā, these should be understood as the names of the letters.

"Lord of Laṅkā, among these, what is the mode of origin of the letters? It is like this:

The Teacher, the supreme secret himself,
Called out to the assembly and spoke:
'Further, if the yogins of the assembly
Infer the meaning by following the letters,
The meaning of the letters will be clearly shown.
Why does meaning itself appear as letters?
From the expanse arises a, and from a
Letters stream forth like wish-fulfilling massing clouds.

a is the letter of intrinsic nature;
Free of any support, it has no cause or condition;
Pervading all from within, it is like space.
Just as the profound, unborn nature of reality
Pervades, abides in, and holds all phenomena,
So, through the nature of a, which pervades and abides,
All letters are pervaded and all are held,
Transformed into various names and phrases.

From the glorious knot at the heart of the Great Being,
The ground of all, from which all things arise,
Dawns into the secret expanse of the throat.
The miracle that appears as five letters,
Sounds arising at the throat, resounding from the throat:
a ka kha ga gha ṅa

As rays of syllables stream forth from a,
The faculty of the tongue streams forth as a half-moon.
The miracle that appears as five letters,
Sounds arising at the tongue, resounding from the tongue:
ca cha ja jha ña

From the tips of the light rays of a
Streams forth the tip of the tongue's half-moon.
The miracle appears as twice five letters,
Sounds arising at the tip, resounding at the crown:
ta tha da dha na
ṭa ṭha ḍa ḍha ṇa

Likewise, from that arises this:
Swirling and blazing from the lips,
Likewise appearing as five syllables,
Arising at the door of the mouth, resounding from the mouth:
pa pha ba bha ma

That very supreme of all syllables
Arises there on the flowing path of speech;
Dawning toward objects as syllables of awareness,
The miracle appears as various letters:
ya va ra la
śa ṣa sa ha
kṣa
a ā i ī u ū e ai o au aṃ a
ṛ ṝ ḷ lī a ho

From the self-arisen, abiding, pervading expanse
Dawn the light rays of the garland of speech.
When fashioned from the wisdom of speech,
The seeds of the guides' emanations arise.
The body, speech, and mind of the countless victors
Are precisely the emanation of the letters.

It is the very mind of the unborn.
The first are the reflections of the throat;
The second are the reflections of the tongue;
The third are those that arise from the tip;
The fourth dawn at the lips where they arise;
The fifth are the flowing path of speech.

ya is the letter that gathers all;
ra is the letter from which burning arises;
la is the letter of great destruction;
vā is the letter that dispels darkness;
śa is the letter that purifies signs;
ṣa is the letter of great emptiness;
kṣa is the letter from which miracles arise;
sa is the letter of all aspects;
ha is the letter that suppresses saṃsāra;
a is the letter of all arising;
vā is the letter of all expression.

The sixteen letters of the immeasurables and the paths are complete.
The four letters of speech, from which arise the words
Of body, speech, mind, and qualities;
And seven and twelve, the flowing of speech:
These the Protector has taught as arising.'

Lord of Laṅkā, these should be understood as the way the letters arise.

Lord of Laṅkā, what here is the form of the letters? It is simply color and shape. Lord of Laṅkā, what is color? It is natural, of a class, various, and indeterminate.

Lord of Laṅkā, shapes are diverse. Thus even for a alone there are different shapes: a like a lion poised, a like a lion stretching, a like a lion roaring, a like a perfect drop, a resembling the mouth of an amokari, a like an öpak line, and so on. Likewise for ka there are different shapes: ka like a lute, ka like a trident, ka like a wolf, ka like a turtle, ka like a three-pointed banner, and so on.

Further, kha is like a donkey. Ga is like a rhinoceros. Gha is like a vase. Ṅa is like a kaṅka bird. Ca is like the beak of a blue jay. Cha is like a parasol. Ja is like a net. Jha is like a great net. Ña is like the top of a banner. Ta is like Jambudvīpa. Tha is like the mouth of a thabura. Da is like the mouth of a ḍākinī. Dha is like the beak of a goose. Na is like the mouth of a tola. Ṭa is like one eating with relish. Ṭha is like a boulder. Ḍa is like a fang. Ḍha is like a bow. Ṇa is like the mouth of a buffalo. Pa is like Mount Meru. Pha is like a garuḍa. Ba is like a marsh bird. Bha is like a khalaktse. Ma is like a begging monkey. Ya is like the new crescent moon. Va is like the mouth of a kartika. Ra is like the mouth of a kuñcita. La is like a crow tearing at flesh. Śa is like a hare. Ṣa is like the mouth of a shanta. Sa is like a begging brahmin. Ha is like a hāraka. Kṣa is like the mouth of a rākṣasa.

I is like crossed hooks supported by a repa. U is like stacked öpaks drawn out by a repa. E is like stacked repas drawn out by an öpak. O is like a strung bow. Oṃ is like an opened parasol. Aṃ is like a sage of steady discipline. Ṛ is like an iron chain drawn by a hook. Ḷ is like a goose drawn by a hook, and so on: each single letter has different shapes.

Accordingly, the drenpa is like a curved hook. The öpak is like a coiled anklet. The repa is like the raised horn of a rhinoceros. The crescent is like the horn of a naro. The drop is like the eye of a litsaktsik. The staff is like a javelin. Such are their various individual shapes. Lord of Laṅkā, these should be known as the shapes of the letters, that is, as the forms of the letters.

Lord of Laṅkā, what is the intrinsic nature of the letters? It is this: whatever is the intrinsic nature of a letter does not become the intrinsic nature of another letter, and no letter can indicate the intrinsic nature of other letters as a synonym. Lord of Laṅkā, what it is that does not become something else is this: the intrinsic nature of a is non-arising, and by no letter other than a alone does one come to know the intrinsic nature of non-arising. Likewise, the intrinsic nature of ka is action, and by no letter other than ka alone does one come to know action. Lord of Laṅkā, thus, although for whatever is designated by a name its actual referent may be known even by stating another synonym of that name, a letter cannot be known in that way; therefore the intrinsic nature of the letters should be understood to be simply unchanging.

Lord of Laṅkā, what is the sound of the letters? It is this: natural sound, transformed sound, combined sound, and derived sound. Of these, what is natural sound? It is this: although the shape, color, language, and convention of a letter differ, the sound designated by the name a, ka, and so on is known just as it is, according to reality. What is transformed sound? It is this: the sound into which any given letter is transformed, by which the meaning and the word become different. What is combined sound? It is this: the meaning that arises when, onto any given shape, it is joined as the form of any given letter. What is derived sound? It is this: the meaning that arises from the sound of whatever word is derived from any given letter.

Lord of Laṅkā, what here is the action of the letters? It is this: multiplying, and making known. Multiplying is this: from nothing arises one; from one, three; then five, forty, a hundred thousand, ten million, and a million, increasing so as to make everything known. Making known is this: reading names, reading words, reading forms, reading meanings, reading sounds, reading one, reading many, reading one as many, reading many as one, and reading one and many as distinct.

Again, Lord of Laṅkā, what here are the doors of the letters? They should be understood as these: the door of letters, the door of words, the door of meaning, the door of vowels, and the door of dhāraṇī. Lord of Laṅkā, what is the door of letters? It is that by which one writes upon something. What is the door of words? It is the letters. What is the door of meaning? It is the collection of words. What is the door of vowels? It is the sixteen letters.

Lord of Laṅkā, what then are the sixteen letters? They should be known simply as gwar and cwang. These should be understood as long and short. From the two, short and long, the six limbs of the vowels should be understood to arise: two arise from the short, two from the long, and two from both together. The two limbs that arise from the short are these: the śata and the gardha. The two limbs that arise from the long are these: the drawn-out and the clipped. The two limbs that arise from both together are these: the cwangkadeu and the pwangkadeu.

Lord of Laṅkā, these six limbs, from the six doors of speech in the realms of the six destinies, when multiplied six by six, should be understood to be transformed into six and thirteen. By multiplying them into sixty, sixty limbs should be understood to arise.

By multiplying the six into sixty, three hundred and sixty limbs should be understood to arise.

Lord of Laṅkā, what is the door of dhāraṇī? It should be understood as this: the dhāraṇī of meaning, the dhāraṇī of words, the dhāraṇī of Dharma, and the dhāraṇī of mantra. Lord of Laṅkā, what is the dhāraṇī of meaning? One should not be separated from the very meaning that a letter shows. What is the dhāraṇī of words? It is whatever word arises from whatever letter. What is the dhāraṇī of Dharma? The letter should be understood as connected with all phenomena and as the door of not apprehending any phenomenon. What is the dhāraṇī of mantra? It is whatever words are powerfully obtained through the door of the letters. Lord of Laṅkā, these should be understood as the doors of the letters.

Lord of Laṅkā, what here are the categories of letters? They are the letters of intrinsic nature and the letters of miraculous display. Of these, what are the letters of intrinsic nature? That everything whatsoever is simply letters. What are the letters of miraculous display? They are the worldly, the transcendent, and the common. What are the transcendent letters? They are oṃ, hūṃ, māṃ, svaṃ, aṃ, haṃ, and a. Lord of Laṅkā, what here are the worldly letters? They are these: the root, the captured, and the renowned. What is the root? It is the six seeds. What are the captured? Simply that all the sounds and languages of beings are captured by letters. What are the renowned? They are those known as the letters of the different worlds: the letters of the gods, the letters of the nāgas, the letters of the asuras, the letters of humans, the letters of the yakṣas, and so on. What are the common letters? They are a, ka, and so on.

Lord of Laṅkā, what here is the basis of the letters? a, oṃ, hūṃ, and mūṃ: these are the basis from which everything arises.

When the Lord of Secrets had said this, all the beings of inferior bodies, the Lord of Laṅkā and the rest, came to understand the wheel of the inexhaustible ornament of speech.

This is the thirty-fourth chapter of the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas, called The Array of the Wheel of the Inexhaustible Ornament of Speech.

Then again the Lord of Laṅkā, the bodhisattva great being, spoke thus to the Lord of Secrets and asked: "Lord of Secrets, how do the qualities and activities of the buddhas that appear from the blessing of secret mantra appear?"

The Lord of Secrets said: "Lord of Laṅkā, the qualities and activities of the buddhas that appear from the blessing of secret mantra should be understood to appear in this way. How? It is like this:

From examining as permanent what is beyond thought
Arises the blessing of thought.
The inexhaustible precious sky treasury,
The great blessing, flows without ceasing.

The blessing of the essence, secrecy itself,
Remains without conceptualization,
Yet performs every deed that arises as wished;
And in this there is no doing at all.

Just as, by the blessing of a single gourd,
A tongue is transformed into a thousand,
So in the instant of seeing the secret meaning
Everything becomes the secret essence.

Just as, by the blessing of a single lamp,
The darkness of an eon is dispelled,
So in the instant of knowing the meaning of the essence
Obscurations that have lasted for eons are dispelled.

Just as one who has a precious jewel
Has no fear of poverty,
So one who has awakened to the meaning of the essence
Has no fear of saṃsāra, the place of falling.

Beside a jewel blazing with the light of sun and moon,
Other jewels are not worth a hundred-thousandth part;
Beside a single instant of secrecy itself,
All merit perfected in whiteness is not a hundred-thousandth part.

However vast the ocean of milk of beings may be,
Just as a single drop of lion's milk poured in destroys it,
So in the ocean of karma, afflictions, and suffering,
By the appearance of secrecy itself for an instant, all is gone.

Just as one who relies on a universal monarch has no fear,
So one certain of secrecy itself does not fear hostile enemies.

Even a broken vajra outshines all jewels,
And, never lost as a jewel, it dispels the suffering of poverty.
Even if secrecy itself, the vajra essence, should decline,
It outshines the śrāvakas and the rest, and the Middle Way,
And the certain appearance of secrecy itself dispels poverty in Dharma.

Wherever the scent of a lion cub spreads,
All who cannot bear it take flight.
Even one without the power of knowing the meaning of the essence
Outshines the Middle Way merely by generating the mind of awakening.
Hostile enemies are overpowered and terrified,
And each, in his own place, is tormented by pain.

A person whose hands and feet are cut off, though maimed, still lives;
But just as with the heart torn out there is no chance of living,
So though the Middle Way, which is like a limb, should decline, one lives,
But without secrecy itself, which is like the heart, one perishes.

Whoever holds the secret, the vajra essence,
Is like one who rides seated in a chariot or on a horse:
Just as there is no other who does his work,
The Victors honor his mount in their midst.

Though one practice for eons the Middle Way and the three doors of liberation,
The Victors do not regard it or honor it.
But even from a mere instant of knowing the meaning of the essence,
The qualities of blessing could not be fully told even by the Victors.

All merit that reaches the limit of whiteness
Is like the dust on the tip of a fingernail;
Generating a single instant in the vajra essence
Is as great as the whole disk of the earth.

What reaches the limit of the white side
Is but a lamp in a dark house;
Knowing a single instant in the vajra essence
Is like the great fire of the eon at the end of time.

The sky that appears in the Middle Way
Is the sky within a crevice;
The sky of the vajra secret
Is like the sky that spans the great elements.

The pacifying deeds performed through waves of blessing
Appear like the eon of emptiness.
The deeds that enrich all things
Appear like the eon of abiding.
The deeds of bringing under control through compassion
Appear like the eon of formation.
The deeds of fierce subjugation
Are like the eon of destruction.

Lord of Laṅkā, these should be understood to appear from the blessing performed by the array of the inexhaustible treasury of qualities and by the waves of activity."

When he had said this, the beings of inferior bodies came to understand that everything appears from the blessing of the supreme secret, suchness itself.

This is the thirty-fifth chapter of the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas, The Array of the Wheel of the Inexhaustible Ornament of Conduct.

From the way of definite emergence of the Great Vehicle: the seventh teaching on the way things arose, the discussion of the wisdom seal that recounts the history of what arises also in the appearance of blessing.

Then again the Lord of Laṅkā, the bodhisattva great being, put a full question to the Lord of Secrets about the eighth of the twelve words given earlier, where it says, "It arose also when it became renowned through transmission by means." He asked: "O Lord of Secrets, when you yourself, teaching how the supreme secret of the Great Vehicle arose, set out your explanation as a historical account, you wrote, 'It arose also when it became renowned through transmission by means.' How was it transmitted, and where did it become renowned?"

He said: "Lord of Laṅkā, it was transmitted by intention, and becoming renowned in words, it came to appear in the worlds of gods and humans. Lord of Laṅkā, what is that? It is this:

Who transmitted what to whom?
'It is suchness itself':
The King of Dharma to his regent
Definitively transmitted the secret intention.
In the abodes of the gods and the world of humans,
What the Lord of Secrets discussed
Had been granted earlier to persons
Renowned as beings who hold inferior bodies.
And how did that renown arise?
In time past, long ago,
When the protector of beings, Vajragarbha,
Was ripening in the field of those to be tamed
The Black One, bound to saṃsāra by his own rope,
To the assembly of the ripened and unripened
That had gathered from every side
He spoke this meaning of the true secret.

Lord of Laṅkā, at that time and in that age, in order that those sons who had not recognized themselves as fully perfected might recognize themselves as fully perfected, he urged them in secret, declaring: 'Great gathered assembly, you who have not recognized that you are fully perfected: in order to recognize that you are fully perfected from the very beginning, take up the uncontrived samaya!'

The gathered assembly requested: 'Blessed One, in order to take up that very thing, what is this called the uncontrived samaya? How should we take it up?'

When they had requested this, the Blessed One declared: 'Great gathered assembly, the uncontrived samaya is this:

The unmistaken, uncontrived samaya
Is to set the mind on the nature of reality, suchness,
And not to stray from it:
That is the samaya of the Victors.'

When he had declared this, the gathered assembly were greatly frightened and terrified and utterly displeased. 'Alas!' they said, 'a lord of illusion has deceived us! He has blocked our various individual inclinations and handed us over to the great emptiness. With this, each of us is parted from our own samaya!' And they wept.

Then the Blessed One declared: 'O assembly of those who carry human skulls and the rest, take heart, and all of you offer your own samayas, whatever pleases each of you. Since nothing at all is blocked in the uncontrived samaya, it appears without obstructing anything.'

When he had declared this, the sons who had been taken in were pleased and delighted. Their radiance returned, and all of them eagerly offered the samayas each delighted in.

The Black Captain and his retinue said: 'Great Hero, our samaya is this:

By our own great austerities
We abide in our own dominion
And crush the factions of others:
That is our samaya.'

Then the lords of the lustful said: 'Maheśvara, our samaya is this:

With mother, daughter, and sister,
With women who may be approached and who may not,
To engage in passion out of desire:
That is our samaya.'

Then the lords of the hateful said: 'Great Terrifier, our samaya is this:

To snatch away the lives
Of each and every sentient being
And to feast on flesh and blood:
That is our samaya.'

Then the chiefs of the yakṣa hosts said: 'Great Terrifier, our samaya is this:

To take from all the lords of wealth
Their gold and other riches
And to enjoy them as we please:
That is our samaya.'

Then those given to conceptual rites said: 'King of wish-fulfilling thought, our samaya is this:

Having cut off someone's skull,
Whoever bears that auspicious sign
Becomes like a wish-fulfilling jewel:
That is our samaya.'

Then the host of the low-born marked with moles said: 'King of perfect dominion, our samaya is this:

Cow, dog meat, and horse meat,
Human flesh, elephant flesh, and the rest:
If one eats them, all qualities are accomplished:
That is our samaya.'

Then the blood-snatching rākṣasīs said: 'King of blood drinkers, our samaya is this:

The warm blood of every living creature
That has been slain,
If drunk, makes one blaze like a leopard:
That is our samaya.'

Then the sorceresses said: 'Lord of great miracles, our samaya is this:

Skull, bone, marrow, oil, and blood,
Mixed with beer and amṛta,
Are the sacred substances that accomplish magical power:
That is our samaya.'

Then the low-born vomit-eaters said: 'Great Fierce and Terrible One, our samaya is this:

Great flesh, feces, urine, semen, and blood
Are the sacred substances where amṛta swirls,
Which destroy all diseases without exception:
That is our samaya.'

Then the host of women said: 'Great Graceful One, our samaya is this:

With dance, song, poetry,
And alluring ways and poses we steal the mind,
And by skill in attending we engage in sex:
These are the samayas of delight.
That is our samaya.'

Then the host of gamblers said: 'Lord of Speech, our samaya is this:

So as to be hard for others to fathom,
We speak fittingly in emanations of speech:
This is the supreme great deception.
That is our samaya.'

Then the host of those with perverse craving said:

'Great True Being, our samaya is this:

If one knows everything within the state of the true,
Then whatever one does, great or small, of the two kinds of action,
One does not come under the sway of the two:
That is our samaya.'

Then the host who rely on secret words said: 'Yogin of the secret, our samaya is this:

Wearing a hundred or a thousand times
Garments of feces, urine, blood, and soot-water,
If one cuts the effigy, even the buddhas are afraid:
That is the secret samaya of secret mantra.'

Then the Blessed One declared the samaya that obstructs nothing, unmixed with anything:

'All you fortunate ones gathered here,
The samaya that is a wish-fulfilling jewel
For your various individual inclinations:
Take it up in its entirety!

Crushing the factions of others
And practicing the five deeds of immediate retribution,
Yet keeping the great self in view:
This, it is taught, is the vessel for accomplishing buddhahood.

With mother, daughter, sister,
And all women who move about,
If one engages in passion out of desire,
That, it is taught, is the vessel for accomplishment.

Those who delight in killing
Cut off the stream of the life faculty,
Eat flesh and bone and drink blood:
This is the true great samaya.

From the treasure mines of the lords of wealth,
Taking gold and the rest without its being given
And enjoying it as one wishes:
This is the true samaya.

For one with the mind of yogic awareness,
Cutting off someone's skull
And knowing the Dharma in the charnel-ground vessel
Is the samaya of true view,
The holy samaya of desire.

Cow, dog meat, and horse meat,
And elephant, the supreme great flesh:
Since they are the food of the great yoga,
Nothing else should be eaten.

Whoever drinks
From the ocean of the great red, of blood,
This is the samaya of all blood drinkers:
Drinking it to the last, one's splendor is great.

Attending all women
With dance, song, and the like,
And engaging in wrong conduct out of desire:
This is the great samaya of the nature of reality.

Because one is hard to fathom,
Saying "it exists" of what is not and "it is not" of what exists,
Telling lies that fit the object:
This is the samaya of the Buddha's word.

To those who from beginningless time
Have wandered, saying "It is their own fault,"
And speaking lies with the eight abuses:
This is the samaya of the Buddha's speech.

Since all distinctions are one,
Not coming under the sway of karma:
This is the unmistaken, uncontrived samaya,
The samaya without ripening.

Unmoving, unchanging, unshakable,
Though one does not apprehend existence or nonexistence by apprehending,
That which is never lost
Is the great samaya of the unmoving.

Since all is one in the great suchness,
There is neither taking up nor rejecting;
All phenomena that appear as two
Are entirely one in the great suchness.

Since in this, whether white or black,
Distinct causes and results
Do not exist, just like space,
This is the great samaya of equality.

From the king, the awareness of sameness,
There are no distinctions of any kind at all;
It is found by oneself and not through another:
This is the samaya of the great self-arisen.

Wearing garments of feces, urine, and defilement,
If one cuts off the continuity of the effigy's births,
And, reciting through some hundred kinds of births,
Joins that very one to that,
So that all the form bodies of the buddhas
Dissolve into the dharmakāya itself,
This is the samaya of vajra mantra.

The five enjoyments in which desire engages:
If one engages in them with passion out of desire,
This is the samaya of the ornament of the nature of reality,
The samaya of great bliss itself.'

When he had declared this, the hosts of the great assembly were utterly pleased and delighted, and all requested with one voice: 'How wonderful! King who fulfills wishes, our hearts are delighted! You are our chief! You are our king! Please take us into the uncontrived samaya!'

When they had requested this, Lord of Laṅkā, the Blessed One declared: 'Aho! So it is! Excellent! It is entrusted! Take up that holy samaya! tiṣṭha vajra samaya stvaṃ.' Thus they were to hold it without straying from it.

Lord of Laṅkā, then again the gathered host requested of the Blessed One: 'Blessed One, since the Blessed One is our lamp, if we abide in these samayas of our own, what family will we hold, and where will we attain our state? Please teach us!'

When they had requested this, Lord of Laṅkā, the Blessed One declared: 'Gathered host, listen, and I shall explain what will happen if you abide in your own samayas.

How wonderful! Host of skull bearers,
These words that you have spoken
Are words bound by knots;
They will never become free.

Saying 'the true, the true':
Even the true itself is a knot.
Unless the knot is untied,
There will be no liberation or release.

Committing the deeds of immediate retribution and the like
And crushing the factions of others
Will not bring liberation:
This is called the work of Māra.

Acts of passion or of sexual union:
Deluded there by the power of passion,
Those beings pass into his city
And become his beings.

Where, out of hatred, the act of killing
Severs the life from someone's body,
One falls into the castes of butchers and slaughterers:
This is the root of suffering.

The Victors are utterly pure;
So when one has served before the face
Of the Sugata, the Victor,
Various despised substances,
Then through the ripening of that karma
One is seized by an illness with no escape
And sinks into a swamp of filthy vomit.

Food such as flesh and blood
Is great stolen bait;
Into the bodies of those who eat it
All the scent-eaters gather
And steal their radiance and their vital essence,
So that their splendor and glow fade away.

While one has a jewel of one's own,
To enjoy and seize the jewel of another:
These ways of wrong living by wrong means
Are tight knots.

Saying, "There is no difference between white and black actions,"
And acting in a motley way in everything,
Is the family-less, the cut-off family, the perverted family;
Its result is to pass into a perverted realm.

If in the acts that please the Sugatas
One is impure in limb and word,
Then because of that cause, the act of wrongdoing,
In every period of time
One's faculties and limbs will be incomplete,
And the essence of mindfulness will decline.

Meditating on and reciting the Sugata Victors
Like a contrived miracle that merely resembles them:
Whoever imagines it so and gains it so
Is a vidyādhara who is a rākṣasa of karma.

All ornaments and all attire:
If someone conceives of them as this and that,
He becomes a vidyādhara in the guise of an ugly brahmin,
And limbs of makaras, fish, and the like arise.

All the concepts of samādhi:
If someone conceives of them as this and that,
Conceiving of anything at all and clinging,
They become great kīlas of grasping.

All the causal syllables,
āḥ, hūṃ, and the rest:
If one conceives of each of them as that,
They are sandalwood, venomous snakes, and the like,
Great obstacles of conceptuality that make one fall.

The five great substances such as blood:
If one conceives of them as mere things,
One takes birth in the class
Of bhūtas who sniff the smell of blood.

If one's conception fixes on the guise
Of the fierce mātṛkās,
They wander in the land of Laṅkā
As rākṣasīs and ugly piśācīs.

Whoever conceives with certainty in that way
Of effigies of signs bearing ransom figures
Will take birth in the class of great predators
Of the toll-gate places and be found there.

If one conceives with certainty in that way
Of hosts of slave women ruled by karma,
One becomes a host of black yakṣiṇīs
Who harm the lives of others.

Therefore this tight knot
Is a load too heavy to carry.
Blinded by wrong view,
One loses the precious jewel of mind
In the realm of permanence and annihilation.'

When he had declared this, the gathered host realized that they were seized by the illness of conceptuality and had strayed from their own samayas. "Alas!" they said, "the lord of illusion has deceived us!" And they fainted and wept.

At that time and in that age, the Blessed One declared: "O sons of noble family, look closely at your own continuums!" When he had declared this, the gathered host looked at what the continuum of their karma was like, and everything appeared like reflections in a mirror.

Lord of Laṅkā, they then requested of the Blessed One: "Alas, Blessed One! If it is certain that our samayas have become wrong austerities, please make these very ones into true austerities. Please make them into austerities of the nature of reality, so that we may practice our own samayas without their being blocked. Why is that? Because we are attached to our own austerities and cling to them. All phenomena are like illusions; they can become anything at all. The door of the Great Vehicle is like the mass of fire at the end of the eon; likewise it is like space, like the great earth, and like the great wind. Since the Buddha, the Blessed One, is lord of an ocean of means, please explain in accord with each one's inclination."

When they had requested this, Lord of Laṅkā, the Blessed One laughed with great delight and said: "O sons of noble family, it is good to ask in this way about the meaning of the Great Vehicle. Yet since even all the buddhas and bodhisattvas give rise to doubt about this, what need is there to speak of you?"

When he had declared this, the gathered host requested: "Blessed One, giving rise to doubt is a lack of certainty. Since the buddhas, the blessed ones, possess certainty, would they speak of what is uncertain here within this Dharma assembly?"

The Blessed One declared: "O gathered host, I say that all phenomena are without certainty. Why is that? Because there is no phenomenon whatsoever that is found to be permanent. Therefore your own samayas too are without certainty. When you took, and when you take, countless births other than this one, each birth was different; just so, your inclinations, karma, fortunes, places, groups, physiques, families, colors, livelihoods, views, and conduct are different. So to which of them will you now hold as certain? For this reason I say that all phenomena are without certainty.

Then the gathered host said, "If that is so, Blessed One, how is it when we hold our samayas as certain?"

The Blessed One declared: "O gathered host, it is like this:

The samaya of the five acts of immediate retribution
Is this: samaya, samādhi, wisdom,
Bodhicitta, and being endowed with compassion,
Never cutting their stream, with no interval between.
This is the samaya of the immediate acts.

The samaya of great passion
Is not to give up wisdom and compassion
Within saṃsāra and the nature of reality.
This is the samaya of passion.

Mother, daughter, and sister
Are the nature of reality, the five wisdoms,
And the self-arisen wisdom;
These are taught to be the dhāraṇī of suchness.

When non-arising is shown to someone,
The stream of births that no longer takes up the life faculty
Is cut off, and all is destroyed into emptiness:
That is what is called killing.

To show things out of what is no thing,
And to declare that what appears as things does not exist,
Is the word of all the victors;
It is the great samaya of deceiving.

To seize the wish-fulfilling jewel of others
With the light rays of devotion
And set it in awakening,
And to take suchness itself with the hook:
This is the samaya of taking what is not given.

Desiring that great suchness,
To turn away from clinging to signs
Is misconduct out of desire;
It is the great samaya of union.

Samaya, samādhi, wisdom,
The nature of mind, and great compassion are five;
Great flesh, feces, urine, semen, and blood:
This joining of the five kinds
Is the requisite of all dharmas.
If one is not endowed with these requisites,
The aims will not be accomplished.

The requisites of wrong livelihood, too,
Views and afflictions, karma and afflictions,
Joined with the seven and the five of desire,
Are proclaimed by that same name,
Yet they become saṃsāra itself.
The five views of wrong conduct
Are known as the five polluted meats;
Thus, since those views are impure,
Measured against the correct view, they are pollution."

When the Blessed One had spoken thus, those gathered hosts, seeing the meaning of non-arising, were utterly amazed and said, "Blessed One, are these samayas of ours not, then, admitted within the Great Vehicle?"

He declared, "The samayas of each of the gathered hosts are not excluded from the complete Great Vehicle."

They said, "Blessed One, please explain why they are not excluded."

The Blessed One declared:
"Just as, in the nature of space,
Appearance and existence appear without being blocked,
So in this space of the Great Vehicle
No view or conduct whatsoever is excluded.
Therefore all is the appearance of space."

When he had said this, the whole gathered host was satisfied and amazed, and they wept. Then the Blessed One, though he knew, declared, "Gathered host, what have you heard that you weep?"

They said, "Blessed One, that you did not teach us before so great a matter of lasting meaning as this shows little compassion!"

To this the Blessed One declared, "Gathered host, each of your own spaces is merely obscured by your own adventitious darkness. When it appears as your own space itself, what need is there for anyone to teach it to anyone?"

When he had said this, whatever had been unaware became aware. They drew in their four limbs, struck their limbs upon the ground, and offered their bodies as seats and thrones; thereby they purified the obscuration of the afflictions, and by listening to the Dharma with their ears they purified the cognitive obscuration. They fully performed the task of being the seats and thrones beneath the feet of the vidyādharas, in bodies of lions, horses, elephants, and the like.

This concludes the thirty-sixth chapter, on dispelling delusion and liberating through concordant conduct, from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

Here the sixth section begins.

Then the Lord of Laṅkā, the bodhisattva great being, questioned the Lord of Secrets, saying, "O Lord of Secrets, please relate in full the narrative of how the supreme secret, renowned as handed down from one to another, was practiced in the past, as it has been told."

The Lord of Secrets said: "Lord of Laṅkā, the narrative of the past, renowned as the practice of the supreme secret, is this. The gathered host said to that Blessed One, the Lord of the Supreme Secret, 'Blessed One, how are we to understand the conduct by which practicing the supreme secret turns out well, and the conduct by which it goes wrong?'

"When they had asked this, Lord of Laṅkā, that Blessed One declared: 'Great gathered host, know that practicing the conduct of the supreme secret mantra is extremely difficult. Why is that? The supreme secret is very much like fire. When fire is kept constantly and used well, it constantly yields what one desires, just as one wishes. Yet that very fire, if misused, becomes a great mass of flame: it burns the one who uses it, it spreads from house to house and burns them too, and all goods and possessions are consumed.

"'Likewise, this secret mantra is like water. When water is held by skillful means, it flows just as one wishes; but for one who is unskilled in the nature of water and in means, the user himself is swept away.

"'Moreover, this supreme secret is like a savage beast of prey. Though a savage beast kills others and destroys others, one who is skilled in means brings it under control just as it should be; but if it is not held by means, it falls upon one's own life.

"'Moreover, this secret mantra is like the precious jewel on a serpent's head. If it is held by means, one may enjoy the delight of that jewel; but if it is not held by means, it robs one of life.

"'Moreover, it is like the henbane plant. If it is held by means, it cures even a violent fever; but if it is not held by means, it parts one from life.

"'So what is held by means, like the elixir that transforms poison, the elixir that transmutes into gold, and the lamp that dispels darkness, is able, though small, to overcome what is great; and through unmistaken certainty everything becomes suchness.

"'Therefore, great gathered host, since this supreme secret is difficult to practice, among those who practiced its conduct in the past there were also some who, not recognizing the supreme secret, went onto a wrong path.'"

"'But there were also those who, recognizing it through skill in means, went onto the unerring path. Therefore, great gathered host, become skilled in practicing its conduct with the great cloud of means that recognizes the supreme secret.'

"When he had declared this, the gathered host said, 'Blessed One, what in this is the wrong path of secret mantra, and what is the unerring path? Please explain it to us at length.'

"When they had asked this, Lord of Laṅkā, that Blessed One declared to the great gathered host: 'Great gathered host, it is like this:

Whatever is practiced as renowned through transmission,
This secret mantra, the heart of the secret,
Must be established just as it is.
This is the true secret path.

Since wrong dharmas
And secret mantra, the heart of the secret, these two,
Share the one sound of a designated name,
Grasp the sign that brings certainty.

Because this secret mantra of the Great Vehicle
Is taught in the language of means that accord with beings,
It may turn into something outer and other;
So do not set the meaning in words and sounds.

This intention of the holy ones
Cannot be realized
By example, by valid cognition, by logical reason,
Or by the wisdom of inference.

Whoever strives to speak
Of the intended meaning, the heart of the secret,
While his mind has not attained it,
Would he not live if he did not speak?

Likewise, those who take up its conduct,
Who practice it and who examine and investigate it,
Without having found certainty about the meaning,
What harm would there be if they did not practice it?

Why? Because in this ocean of secret mantra,
As with the spray scattered by a sneeze,
Even in each single word of the holy ones' speech
Many and various meanings arise.

Therefore whoever speaks
Without realizing the meaning of the supreme secret mantra
Commits the sin of false speech, heavier
Than that of one who has done the five acts of immediate retribution.

Those who go astray regarding the secret itself,
Who proclaim the secret with perverse craving,
Will arise and steer others wrongly, saying,
"If one is held by the rite of secret mantra,
Even if one cuts off a living being's life,
That does not become a fault."

If you ask what that is like,
It is like what came about in times past:
The brahmins of perverse craving for livelihood,
False speakers who held sway over the practice of causes.

Where wrong conduct spreads here,
Everyone enters into wrongness itself;
Labeling that with the word "secret,"
They make secret mantra into sorcery.

If you ask what that is like,
It is like what came about in times past:
Those of perverse craving in whom something other arose,
Who practiced another thing resembling it but not it.

Where, in this way, wrong dharmas
Take the words and sounds of the supreme secret mantra,
Which are one and yet not one, each distinct,
Whoever practices in the darkness of delusion,
That mantrin does the work of Māra
And after death goes to the great hell.

Why? Because, not held by the view,
They practice the conduct of māras and rākṣasas
With delight and pleasure, and indulge in every desire;
By the force of their clinging to those habitual tendencies
They fall into the great Vajra Hell,
Beyond even the eight burning hells.

If you ask what that is like,
It is like what came about in times past:
The wonder-workers endowed with perverse powers,
Crowned with the suffering of perverse clinging.

Ah, beings are not set free!
Why? Because in times to come,
Through the fabricated secret words of the deceitful,
The stainless secret teachings
Will be stirred up and altered, the word corrupted and the scriptures cast aside,
And meaningless wrong dharmas will spread.
They do not come from anywhere else;
They merely arise from the root itself.

If you ask what that is like,
It is like what came about in times past:
The corrupters through whom this was not passed on in between,
Who, by their evil lineage, corrupted that very thing.

Because this wish-fulfilling jewel of mine
Shows itself as anything at all and is hard to fathom,
In future times, through wrong conduct,
Many wrongly conceived, wrong schools will arise.

If you ask what that is like,
It is like what came about in times past:
The wrong path, the empty path, the evil path,
The lineages of the six wrong schools of perverse clinging.

Those who, under the sway of perverse clinging,
Hold perverse seeing foremost with perverse minds,
Do not enter any of the doors of the view,
And remain fixed in deluded views.

If you ask what that is like,
It is like what came about in times past:
Those whose minds cling solely to wrong view,
The Black Carakas, deluded in everything.

Those here who, bound by the five bonds,
Seize upon the means of their own greatness,
And do not engage in even the least examination,
Will arise like a deluded, maddened elephant.

If you ask what that is like,
It is like what came about in times past:
Those who proclaim the self alone as Dharma,
Who practice a single wrong side alone.

Just as one born blind who sets out on a road,
Gauging the measure of his steps with his mind,
Runs along a perilous path,
So those will arise who take mere guesswork as paramount.

If you ask what that is like,
It is like what came about in times past:
Those seized by darkness,
The fivefold school holding ascetic vows as supreme.

Meditating on yoga without scripture,
Proclaiming what is not Dharma to be Dharma,
And, in the mad ascetic vows of sinners,
Practicing glorious austerities to excess: this will arise.

If you ask what that is like,
It is like what came about in times past:
Those who have not measured by valid cognition,
The four schools of those who speak nonsense.

Striving at meaningless sitting,
And holding cast images and other likenesses
As the signs of the object of focus:
There a mountain of such reasoning will arise.

If you ask what that is like,
It is like what came about in times past:
Those who blocked all movement,
The seven schools of meditative absorption.

Those who turn the hidden meanings into mere sounds
And make them mere matters for analysis
Do not land on high but go astray;
They are hair-splitters of enlightened body, speech, and mind.

Lord of Laṅkā, in this way
Did that supreme secret one explain it.
Moreover, one endowed with means acted like this.
Then, among those gathered in that assembly,
To one called Light of Brahmā,
Who practiced the supreme vehicle of yoga,
A yogin called Renowned,
Who practiced with lesser inclination,
Earnestly put this question:

"Light of Brahmā, you who practice the supreme,
Saying, 'Here I practice the supreme,'
You practice exceedingly low acts.
Since their remedies are different,
Darkness does not become light;
How could the low become the supreme?

It is like someone wishing to go to a high place
Who goes down a path that slopes downward;
Wishing to see the great state,
You enter the stream of the terrible lower realms.

Just as one who wants water to drink
Is never satisfied by chasing a mirage,
Wishing the low to ripen into the supreme
Does not come about by practicing the low in the name of equality.

Though a garuḍa may brood a serpent's egg,
It cannot be turned into its opposite
And appears just as what it is;
So the low cannot become the supreme.

Those here who have turned away from the secret,
Who practice low and wrong conduct,
Say, 'Since all is empty, without intrinsic nature,
Affliction and awakening are equally empty.'

Medicine that sustains life and poison that kills
Have a single cause, being produced by the elements,
Yet their power to sustain life or to kill
Appears as distinct and opposed.

So would anyone here,
Saying, 'Set in motion by the elements, they are one and equal,'
Go so far as to eat and drink poison?
Likewise, though supreme and low acts
Are of one meaning in being without intrinsic nature,
Would one, saying 'They are equal,' set aside the supreme
And deliberately practice the low?

Thus, if you say that to make no distinction
Between the supreme and the low, the pure and the impure, the great and the small,
Good and bad, happiness and suffering, and the like,
As places to rely on and as things to practice,
Is the conduct that marks the union of equality,
How could poison and food, happiness and suffering,
Which are unequal, be leveled into equality?

In the uncontrived, equal nature of reality,
Things are made unequal by adventitious karma;
Yet stainless space is clear from the very beginning,
And this is like wishing to wash it to make it pure.

Medicine that overcomes illnesses
Becomes poison for those who are not ill.
The means for guiding a world of wrong views,
If practiced as the real thing, are certainly at fault.

The conduct that engages in accord with beings is a means of guidance,
Practiced for the sake of those one guides.
Just as one endowed with strength and knowledge
Corrects what is discordant and makes it concordant,
So the holy ones, whose words accord and are full of meaning,
Ascertain things just as is fitting.

Just as one who, from timidity, greedy for gain,
And wanting fame, without real confidence,
Enters battle and contends in strength and skill,
Falls into others' power and is destroyed,
So one who feigns being supreme,
Out of conceit about gain and fame,
Takes the concordant conduct meant to guide those of perverse craving
To be with certainty the true supreme,
And practices the very path of words as mere sound,
Certainly ruins himself, destroys others, and is low.

Apart from the measure of the guideline for all,
The measure of understanding, realization, and capacity,
And the measure of taming in each case,
If one passes beyond measure, there is no object;
Having passed beyond, one is simply lost.
Whoever passes beyond measure,
Just as even food turns into poison,
Is a madman eating deadly poison;
Even for the holy ones this is not fitting.

Those who, while not freed or liberated
From whatever binds them,
Claim to liberate all of everything:
What a great deception that is!

If one has not known that very thing and is not freed,
And one's mind has not become pure,
Even if one were set on the ground of a buddha,
What one sees there is the abode of hell.
If one knows it and possesses its meaning,
Those great hells are pure realms.

Though the heart of the secret pervades all,
If one does not know it and is not endowed with it,
It is like showing forms to the blind:
The darkness of delusion and of saṃsāra."

When he had earnestly asked this,
The sublime yogin of the Great Vehicle
Called Light of Brahmā
Spoke words of certainty about the supreme
To the one practicing yoga with lesser inclination,
Called Endowed with the Manner of Renown:

"You here who hold to lesser inclination,
Called Holder of the Manner of Renown:
This secret mantra, the heart of the secret,
Is the domain of none but the buddhas themselves,
And so it is not accomplished by mere outward likeness.

If here one is able in faith, devotion, and aspiration,
Then looking at that itself, in that, as that,
And joining that to that, is that itself;
Just this much is the measure of the vehicle.

The many vehicles taught here
Are not separate things;
To guide those of different inclinations,
Many doors of means are taught.
Since all of them are applied as means of mindfulness
That do not block any inclination,
One does not set the mind of certainty on them all;
Certainty lies in the one alone.

Because it is easy, holding all and entering all,
It is called the Great Vehicle.
Because the vehicle is great and its means many,
Nothing whatsoever is blocked.
Not blocking, its path of entry is easy;
Being easy, it is easy to practice.

Though the distinctions of means and vehicles
Are separate, discordant, and different,
Since one practices the certainty of means,
In certainty itself there is no difference.

Samaya, sādhana, and conduct,
And all the various and different views;
Animals, pretas, and nonhumans;
Foul vomiting, deliberate acts, and the like:
All these, looked at perversely,
Are in order to draw beings into the supreme meaning.
Though they are of various and different kinds,
Intending mind itself as one in the vajra,
In order to accord with all the various ones,
They are merely applied as means to join, to attend, to entice, to guide,
And to lead.

To guide the low of all the world
In various ways to the supreme,
He merely brings them to full completion
Through endless low views and conduct.

Though a serpent's egg does not become a garuḍa,
Like the elixir that turns things to gold by transmutation,
If one knows, uncontrived, the suchness
Of all that is low, it becomes supreme.

In the very expanse of suchness
There is no high or low of lower realm and lofty state.
Though fault, delusion, and awakening differ,
In emptiness those two do not exist.
Though poison and medicine are separate,
In the elements those two do not exist.
Though unmixed, various, and different,
The nature of all is fully complete.

If here one is certain to become supreme,
One deliberately practices low acts.
Those who have not seen that very thing,
Following the path of verbal convention,
Stray from the true path
And arise as nothing but followers of a wrong path."

Lord of Laṅkā, in this way
The one called Light of Brahmā
Cut through the doubts in the small question
Of the one endowed with the manner of renown.

When that Lord of Secrets had said this to the Lord of Laṅkā, beings of lesser inclination there were definitively liberated into the Great Vehicle itself.

This concludes the thirty-seventh chapter, on cutting through the doubts in the questions, from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

Then again the Lord of Secrets spoke these words to the Lord of Laṅkā, the bodhisattva great being: "O Lord of Laṅkā, at the time of my earliest memory, my teacher, the Lord of the Supreme Secret, told within that holy retinue the renowned narrative of the transmission of the secret, as follows: 'Lord of Secrets, it is like this:

Once, in my contemplation,
Lord of Secrets, I saw that the unerring nature
Of these meanings had declined.
Though most practice mere contrived forms and paths
As though they were the meaning,
I did not see them certain of the meaning;
All of them practice a wrong path.

If you ask in what way they are wrong:
Not abiding in this, falling away from this,
Turning against this and practicing wrong ways,
Taking what is not the way, being uncertain here,
Or perversely contriving, by deceit and guile, a likeness of the truth.

If you ask what that is like,
It is like what came about in times past:
In the eon called Fully Arrayed,
After the buddha called King of Manifest Arising
Had passed into nirvāṇa,
The yogin Endowed with Excellence who then appeared
Taught a contrived and wrong mantra
To his retinue of ignorant, childish people;
And so he, together with his retinue of five hundred,
Strayed from the meaning and turned back into saṃsāra.
Such ways will arise: do not practice them.

Moreover, Lord of Secrets, listen:
At times when the authoritative masters of yoga
Of this wondrous heart of the secret
For the most part are not present,
The ignorant, given to evil notions,
Misconceive it, corrupt its meaning, and cast aside its scriptures;
Erring, they bring themselves to ruin,
And saying, "This is it; others have not realized it,"
Praise themselves and disparage others: this is wrong.

If you ask what that is like,
In the time of that same eon,
A yogin called Śrīdatta,
Whose understanding had not opened to the meaning,
Being unskilled in the methods of counsel,
Cast aside the scriptures of certain understanding
And practiced what is not Dharma as Dharma.
To others possessed of certain counsel
He said of such uncertain understanding,
"This is my understanding,"
And indulged in great manifest pride.
This is the wrong path of existence.
Such ways will arise: do not practice them.

Moreover, Tamer of the Harmful, it is like this:
Those who, remaining in the wake of a former lineage,
Are installed as masters though lacking confidence,
And, under the power of their arrogance in pursuing words,
Act the part of cotton-clad yogins though they do not know,
Pretenders with crooked minds,
Are sinners, fools with the methods of madmen.

If you ask what that is like,
It is like what came about in times past:
In that same eon renowned as Fully Arrayed,
A yogin who held to contrived ways,
Called Stainless Conduct,
Was authorized by learned ones of former times.
A retinue of a hundred thousand
Laid the heavy, great burden of words
Upon that meaningless yogin
Who had come in the wake of the lineage,
And installed him as master, holder of the family lineage;
Greater and greater grew his arrogance.
Though ignorant, he put on a show,
And by practicing a wrong dharma that only resembled the true,
He fell, with his retinue, into the state of an arrogant Rudra.
Such ways will arise: do not practice them.

Moreover, Tamer of the Harmful, Lord of Secrets:
Fearing that their fields, those who give them friendship or enmity,
And the retinue by whose gain and honor they live,
Might come under another's power,
They divide them into separate factions and groups, saying,
"These are the fields where I live
By the wealth of those I tame;
Let none of them join that one here,"
And they stand guard at the door with words of affliction: this is error.

If you ask what that is like,
It is like what came about in times past:
In that same renowned eon,
One called Nashina,
Together with a retinue of five hundred yogins,
Saying, "We greatly practice the most secret mantra,"
Dwelt in solitude in a range of wilderness.
All those who dwelt
In those great valleys
He treated as a field for his flattering schemes.
A yogin endowed with eyes, who belonged to another group,
Saw the meaning and came there for that purpose,
And began to teach at length
The very path of certainty of the ultimate.
But Nashina, claiming the wilderness range as his solitude,
Stood as a wrong, afflicted doorkeeper:
"This region is my field;
You there, get out now!"
By dividing his own faction from others,
He fell onto the path of affliction and saṃsāra.
Such ways will arise: do not practice them.

Moreover, what was meant to be dedicated
To the holy yogins who tame the hard to tame
They take as their own, indulge their desires, and hoard;
One mind draws out another,
And their wrong conduct never attains anything.

If you ask what that is like,
It is like what came about in times past:
In that same renowned eon, Fully Arrayed,
A yogin called Auspicious Bliss,
With his retinue full of attachment,
Followed after the sound of the mantra words.
A woman called Excellent Ornament,
Who had joined that retinue of his,
Was put in charge of the provisions;
Hoarding out of avarice,
She took the body of a dog among the pretas
And wandered forever in a lowly body.
Such ways will arise: do not practice them.

Moreover, Lord of Secrets, Tamer of the Harmful,
The yogin who practices wrongly,
Of wrong views, brazen, savage, and greatly wrathful,
Though his sinful conduct is devoid of meaning,
Says, "I alone am the certain one":
A reckless talker, a thief of the Word, a deceiver, a corrupter.

If you ask what that is like,
It is like what came about in times past:
A yogin who protected the Dharma,
Called Pramata,
Not seeing the meaning or the path,
Became widely renowned to all, saying,
"I alone am the guide."
Thief of the Victor's word, by the ripening
Of his having deceived whatever beings he did,
He sank into the lower realms;
And when freed from there, he became a worldling of wrong views
And fell into a state of practicing self-grasping.
Such ways will arise: do not practice them.

Moreover, Lord of Secrets, tamer,

Those whose sworn vows have lapsed and who are dull to the meaning,
Because their renown makes them lay claim to it,
And who wish to win a king's throne,
Are shameless, brazen, corrupt emanations of darkness.

If you ask what that is like,
It is like what came about in times past:
In that same renowned eon
There appeared a yogin who practiced the way of mantra,
Called Penapaya, with a following of a hundred.
His root vows lapsed and he was dull to the meaning;
With the pride of great arrogance,
Feigning to possess the meaning,
He took up in a big way the statecraft of kings.
As a mountain crushes living creatures,
So by the mountain of pride and arrogance,
The evil karma of saṃsāric conduct,
He fell into the body of a pig, the creature of delusion.
Such ways will arise: do not practice them.

Moreover, Lord of Secrets, listen:
Those who, without having grasped the very meaning of equality,
Bring forth the verbal path of "equality"
And openly indulge in both attachment and aversion,
Are yogins who by their karma make the path of saṃsāra.

If you ask what that is like,
It is like what came about in times past:
In that same renowned eon,
One called Giver of Bliss,
With a retinue of a thousand yogins,
Saying, "This is certainly the supreme secret,"
Indulged in attachment and aversion alike in those he relied on and those he led.
Through the full ripening of that very conduct
He fell into the realm of the rākṣasas.
Such ways will arise: do not practice them.

Moreover, Lord of Secrets, it is like this:
Yogins of small intelligence,
When other yogins discuss the meaning,
Grow afraid and terrified of it;
Fainthearted, displeased, they flee: fools.

If you ask what that is like,
It is like what came about in times past:
In that same renowned eon,
One called Devadatta,
At the time when he was a beginner in the activity of yoga,
When many other yogins of the definitive meaning
Discussed the definitive meaning, the meaning of the secret,
Fled from it, Devadatta with his retinue.
With a mistaken mind, jealousy arose in him;
He abandoned his own place and spoke ill of them.
Thereby, for countless eons,
He strayed from the true meaning and wandered in wrong ways.
Such ways will arise: do not practice them.

Moreover, Lord of Secrets, listen:
To shoot sharp, venomous, poisoned arrows
At yogins who are yogins in truth,
Is to be a yogin who destroys the samaya, mighty as a fortress:
Such a one is his own obstructor.

If you ask what that is like,
A yogin called Mati,
Holy in understanding, came
To where the yogin called Abhaya
Practiced meaningless contrivance.
Out of malice toward that patient one,
Abhaya slandered him and spoke words of reproach;
Thereby Abhaya fell into the Avīci hell.
Such ways will arise: do not practice them.
