Splendor radiating rays, a la la!
Gatherer of all the infinite, a la la!
Burner of all, a la la!
Great devourer, a la la!
Without edge or center, a la la!
Compassion, Cakrasaṃvara, a la la!
Īśvara, the Terrifying, a la la!
Through the marvelous blessing of the supreme secret,
The great nature of fire,
Please explain it to us."

When the assembly had requested this,
He himself, delighted, spoke these words:
"Yogins of the great assembly,
The nature of the vajra fire
I shall explain definitively; listen with respect.
Through this supreme path, especially unsurpassed,
By applying bliss as the yogic means,
All the Victors are pleased, all is burned and purified,
Purified into the supreme activity that accomplishes aims.
In the fire pit of things as they are,
Upon the kindling of things in all their multiplicity,
The fire of suchness arises,
And everything, everything, is burned, purified and dissolved.
In the pure fire pit of the unborn,
Upon the kindling of the four kinds of birth,
The unborn fire free of elaboration arises,
And the habitual tendencies of signs are burned, purified and dissolved.
In the fire pit beyond thought,
Upon the inconceivable kindling,
The fire of Amitābha arises,
And infinite things, numerous as atoms, are burned, purified and dissolved.
In the pure fire pit of the nature of reality,
Upon the kindling of various phenomena,
The fire of the primordial wisdom of emptiness blazes,
And the habitual tendencies toward things are burned, purified and dissolved.
In the pure fire pit of mind itself,
Upon the kindling of sentient beings and migrators,
The fire of self-arisen primordial wisdom blazes,
And the habitual tendencies of saṃsāra are burned, purified and dissolved.
In the fire pit of the bhaga of the ground of all,
Upon the kindling of the five afflictions,
The fire of the five primordial wisdoms arises,
And the concepts of apprehended and apprehender are burned, purified and dissolved.
In the fire pit of the space of appearance and existence,
Upon the kindling of the four elements,
The fire of the blazing mudrā arises,
And the things of appearance and existence are burned, purified and dissolved.
In the fire pit of the four kinds of activity,
Upon the kindling of four and four,
The fire of the four blazing families arises,
And the substances of the sacrifice are burned, purified and dissolved.

"This too, according to the distinctions of each,
In direction, color, shape and substance,
Is to be applied as is fitting.
East, south, west and north; white, yellow, red,
Dark blue, black and ash-colored are applied.
Round, with a lotus of four sections;
The wheel; square and eight-cornered for increase;
A half-moon, truncated and bow-shaped, for magnetizing;
Triangular, the space of the bhaga, for the fierce rite.
Three-tiered, beautiful with radiance and designs,
Adorned with the kindling and substances of the rite,
Endowed with the activity lineage of the wisdom vajra,
Apply the supreme means of the three masters.
Holding the supreme that delights the pure limit,
With secret vajra songs and dances,
Apply the abundant assemblies of the appointed offerings.
Apply the blessing of the vajra activity.
One who possesses the secret sequence of activity,
Having set out in order the sequence of peaceful deities,
And explained the sequences for increase and magnetizing,
Should make complete the deities, the mantras, the mudrās,
And likewise the sequence of songs.
The five lineages of the families,
Each set out in order in its own family,
In the fire pit swirling with the vast expanse,
With sacred substances and blazing nectar,
Are fit to be burned, in order to purify and to attain.
The immeasurable, ocean-like assembly of the Victors,
And the great qualities of the mantra practitioners,
Having been proclaimed in vajra song,
Undertake the great means of pleasing them.
When the acts of pleasing are complete,
Whatever great aims have not yet been purified,
Whether they are accomplished or not,
The wise should examine in the fire.
The two sounds of the fire, its two colors,
Its two modes of blazing, and its six shapes:
These should be thoroughly known.
If the signs are unattractive,
Since these are unpleasing signs,
Expand the rite with a great burning purification.
Then pleasing and desired results arise:
Of the desired signs in the fire,
Take whichever of the three kinds is accomplished.

"Great assembly, furthermore,
When burning and purifying the substances of the corpse,
Having struck the corpse with mantra,
With various most fragrant things
Anoint the corpse and wash it.
Smearing cloth with the essence of the great melted nectar,
Wrap the corpse in it.
On whatever places of the corpse there are,
Having arranged all the applications of mantra,
Seal them with the signs of the mudrās.
Whatever arrangement of the Victors' maṇḍala there is,
Its emblem mudrās,
Wherever they are arranged, in that very place
Set the heart mantras in the same number.
For the heart mantras, the individual,
Unerring root syllables
Are written on cloth or wood and set in place.
When the preparation of the corpse is complete,
All over the outside and back of the corpse
Arrange all of afflicted saṃsāra:
On cloth or on bark,
Set out all the seeds of saṃsāra.
The five afflictions together with the three poisons,
The four births, the five karmas and the six classes of beings,
Having made them into names and forms,
Are to be set out each in its own place.
Seeds, formations and the five things,
Together with the names of apprehended and apprehender,
Having been arranged as symbols,
Are to be set out so as to cover the corpses.
Then that body of the corpse,
Supported on a platform of planks,
Should be placed on top by the wise.
The rite of the hearth, too:
On a maṇḍala of suitable circumference,
Dig and make the rim according to tradition.
The palaces where the mudrās reside
Are divided into their separate areas with lines.
On top of that, build up the firewood.
Its shape is filled in according to the four families,
Its corners firmly supported by pillars,
Three-tiered, with an upper story and a middle chamber,
The lowest filled with kindling packed tight.
On the planks of the middle chamber,
The corpse's face is displayed toward the four directions.
In the upper story above the corpse,
The maṇḍala of mudrās drawn on cloth and the like
Is to be clearly set in place.
Opening four windows in the corpse chamber,
With four cords of vajra dhāraṇī thread,
Having tied them, offer them into the master's hands.
When the work with the wood is complete,
On the platform at its outer edge
Set out the deities of the four kinds of activity.
When the platform has been completed,
Established in particular through the vajra activity,
Upon the master, the Vajra King,
Confer the authority to burn saṃsāra,

With the unwavering, one-pointed great fire,
Continuously perform the gathering and radiating for the person.
Applying the activities of the vajra mantra,
Confer the authority of the four kinds of activity,
And bring the garland of rites to completion.
A master, a holy vajra one,
Having taken up the tally of the corpse's faults,
Counts what was amassed through karma, and proceeds.
The vajra master who conducts the occasion
Should continuously make unsurpassed offerings.
With dance, with songs, and with great rhythmic steps,
Present offerings and music of every kind.
Make the aspiration of vajra activity;
Do this at the end of the rite, at the time of offering.
The one who holds the register of vajra activity,
Taking up the register of the white tally of deeds,
Offers the sum of the good to the holy ones.
The one who holds the tally of the corpse's faults
Burns the black marks as firewood
In the expanse of the fire of primordial wisdom.
The deities abiding in the fire for the fire rite,
The buddha of fire, Amitābha,
And the goddess of fire, Pāṇḍaravāsinī,
Are the light of all the Victors.
They hold mastery over the element of fire,
And are the lords of the warmth of all things.
They have pledged to burn the three realms of existence.
Their enlightened activity of burning things
They do not transgress, by the power of their sacred commitment.
They know precisely whose obscurations are burned.
Whether or not the corpse's consciousness has been purified,
One skilled in fire should examine accordingly.
If unpleasant signs arise, perform the burning purification.
If the signs are good, confer the fire empowerment.
With the ashes, the bone remains and the relics,
Make maṇḍalas of mudrās and the like.
If it was a holy being, enshrine the remains
And offer them as a support of the master.
This is the supreme of all practices.
Thus spoke that supreme being.

Friends, these should be fully understood through the fire rite.

Vajra brothers and sisters in the secret Dharma,
That which comes from the fire rite,
The bone remains known as śarīram:
From immeasurable ages, for as long as time has been,
After countless emanated Victors
Have passed into nirvāṇa,
The bodily remains that arise from nothing,
Called bodily remains and relics,
Are renowned as supports for the merit of beings.
Since the Victors are without substantial existence,
Though bodily remains and relics are impossible,
For the sake of benefiting sentient beings
They set forth, out of nothing, a miracle of compassion.
Likewise, wherever and whatever lamps have arisen
And appear here in this world,
Their remains after nirvāṇa as well
Come to be known as śarīram,
And their function is the same.
Those Victors and others,
Together with the self-arisen ones and those who remain after them,
Having become one in harmonious manner,
Their bodily remains, seeds blessed with power,
Multiply as elixir from portions of their qualities,
And appear pervading the land of Jambudvīpa.
Because they come from bone, they are hard, firm and round,
And conventionally they are well known as vajras.
Some are small pods, three-cornered, like sesame pods.
When young, they appear as butter or as milk;
When grown, the sign of ripening is that they are hard and firm,
Like the grains of the chickpea,
White, with the clear color of red sandalwood.
The benefits of eating them are inconceivable;
They are renowned as the supreme nectar medicine.
Likewise, the vidyādhara heroes
Emanated in human bodies,
Individuals on the grounds who possess the Dharma eye,
Pure fields of merit and glory:
After their bodily frame has been purified into its essence,
What appears to the fortunate
Is, to some, śarīram,
And to some, relic bodies.
To the unfortunate they do not appear.
And even when they do appear to some,
For those who practice they remain long and lasting,
While for the impure they wane and flee away.
They show the omens of what is to come,
Both pleasing and displeasing.
They are not permanent as existent things,
Nor are they destroyed into nonexistence.
Yoga practices and prosperity
Accord with the mode of purity or impurity.
Moreover, if through pure merit
One has vast devotion to the path,
If one is helmeted in one-pointed yoga,
If one is bound by the vajra and one's karma is in accord,
They descend upon those whose mindstreams are completely pure.
Further, in the order of their distinctions:
The śarīram, the bodily remains of the Victors,
Pervading all the buddha fields,
As if rounded by a smith or a craftsman,
Appear though no one has made them,
White and shining, clear in color, like drops,
Luminous with the radiance of red sandalwood,
And fall like rain, filling the sky.
The remains of emanated vidyādhara heroes
Arise white and clear, only a few here and there.
The great signs of individuals on the grounds
Are of even color: one, three, two, three, one;
In some cases five or ten appear,
And such are their qualities.
They too are hard and firm like a vajra;
Even an iron pestle cannot break them.
And the benefits of their arrival:
In all those lands and regions,
Wherever they descend,
Illness, calamity and obstructors are pacified.
Even savage yakṣas and rākṣasas,
Wrathful and fierce, become calm and gentle.
Strife among kings, disturbance of the elements,
And turmoil in the land are pacified.
Fruits and crops increase and ripen,
And everything pleasing comes about.
In that land, the seed of commoners and kings
All becomes sons.
Sons who are happy, bearing the marks, of high lineage,
All of these will be born.
Those who teach these things to others
Share equal fortune with the level of the vidyādharas.
Their merit in this world, too,
Equals that of the universal monarchs (cakravartins).
All their intentions will be fulfilled;
Long life, restoration and their activities will be accomplished.
From then on, this is the great sign
Of touching the ground of unsurpassed awakening.
Besides this, their qualities are inconceivable:
These are the qualities that abide
In their own intrinsic nature.

If one practices with them, their qualities are inconceivable.
Since in sentient beings of every kind
The very essence of awakening is present,
When the five aggregates are purified
Through the work of the fire rite, it is impossible that none should appear.
As for how these are to be practiced:
Gather them inside a crystal casket,
Seal it with the seal of the mudrā,
And set it inside a crystal amulet box.
The practitioner should make aspirations to them,
And, to purify obscurations and to restore, perform the radiating and gathering.
The rite for the radiating and gathering is this:
From the Victors who fill the buddha fields,
Śarīram filling the sky
Fall upon it like a stream of rain.
With one-pointed recitation, a stream of speech,
mahā śarīram gyili hūṃ
Recite it countless times without interruption.
They become supremely clear and shining,
With garlands of light rays like the rays of the sun,
And appear scattered in profusion.
Outside the casket
They will appear clearly.
They move and multiply abundantly,
And Victors, heroes and vidyādharas,
As reflections in the crystal and out of it,
Will clearly show themselves to me.
The first sign is that the vessel is filled;
The second, that half of it is, or a hundred and eight;
Even at the least, they increase by some number.
These are the great signs of accomplishment;
At that time one should receive the siddhis.
The qualities attained are inconceivable:
In this very life one abides on the level of a vidyādhara
And becomes equal to the Victors.
As for the qualities that arise incidentally,
One who acts for the benefit of all beings
Will succeed like a great being.
One must make offerings to them continually;
If one makes offerings, excellent qualities arise.
The rite for making the offerings is this:
Having purified obscurations and generated a mass of merit,
And having made one's own mindstream pure,
At every time of the moon
When its sixteenth digit is full,
Having made oneself alone the focus of the mind,
Make the offering: with the casket,
Tie them to the top of a victory banner,
And make aspirations for whatever one wishes.
By this, all wishes are fulfilled.
For the fortunate yogin, that is
The precious wish-fulfilling jewel.
As the supreme substance for yogic practice,
One must not be without it even in name.
Just as corpses are turned into gold,
And just as they are purified by being burned in fire,
However much one engages with things,
One does not waver from that holy reality itself.
That and other boundless conduct,
Conduct that abides in the four things,
Is the very nature of Vajrasattva.
Of the holy and wondrous conduct:
Just as an elephant that has entered the water,
Without obstruction, attachment or fear,
In the manner of one who joyfully delights in enjoyment,
Like one who has gone beyond, never to turn back,
So too the yogin's definitive conduct,
Entering the waters of the ocean of discipline,
In the joyful manner of one training great prowess,
Crosses over without obstruction or attachment.
As for the means of making one's conduct complete,
What matters is that familiarity becomes the expanse.

Friends, these should be understood as the crossing over.

This was the seventy-first chapter, called Entering Yogic Conduct, from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

Then again the Lord of Secrets spoke to those of lesser capacity: O friends, that by which one is to cut through is this: the holy intention, suchness. Why is that? By training in the single suchness of the intention, one has trained in inconceivable dharmas. If one is skilled in the single suchness, one is skilled in the inconceivable. If one is familiar with that suchness, one meditates on the inconceivable. If one does not transgress that suchness, one has kept the inconceivable sacred commitment. If one enters that suchness, one has entered the inconceivable door. If one engages in that suchness, one enjoys inconceivable requisites. If one actualizes suchness, one has done as much as an inconceivable ocean. If one teaches that suchness without error, one has taught immeasurable doors of the Dharma. If one hears that suchness without distortion, one has heard inconceivable doors of the Dharma. If that suchness becomes manifest, one will see immeasurable Victors. Why is that? Because what is to be made the quintessence or the essence of all of them is suchness, and there is nothing other than it.

Friends, therefore it is like this:
If one sees the meaning of suchness,
Then, by that seeing, all is illusion.
If that state itself is the supreme and noble one,
All unsurpassed heights and dignities are false.
From the moment one is certain of suchness,
There is no discouragement or shrinking before whatever is lesser.
If one has come to a decisive conclusion on suchness,
Though dwelling in the world, one is the dharma body.
Though certain of the holy, one engages with the world,
And has no weariness even at the very limits of saṃsāra.
Though it is boundless, it is perfected as one.
One is not daunted by the realm of the māras,
And not afraid of the unbearable realms.
The things of desire are joined with the expanse.
If one sees the definitive meaning all at once,
Gradual practice is like deceit and enticement.
Even through mere devotion to it
One will outshine the three realms;
What need is there to speak of its having become the expanse?
For example, a lion cub or a garuḍa chick,
Though its eyes are not yet open and it is taken up by its mother,
Terrifies all, like one that goes straight from the egg into the sky.
Those who have become certain of that very reality,
Though they dwell and engage in the world,
In that very holy state
Are without fear of anything at all.
Those who practice with signs in stages,
And, with nonexistence, with object and without object,
With accomplishing and with traversing,
Enter into conceptual thought with no discernment,
Those fools are empty;
How could they be certain of the holy?
Like the young of the lowest beasts of prey,
Their nature is to be frightened and terrified.
Just as the intrinsic nature
Of all the boundlessly many phenomena abides,
Uncontrived and without distortion,
That is coming to a decisive conclusion on suchness.
Just as the great garuḍa soaring in the sky
Ranges the heights of the sky by the strength of its wings,
And though mountains, crags and rivers are boundless,
All are encompassed beneath the beat of its wings,
So the yogin soaring in the sky of the nature of reality,
Though the doors of conduct of the vehicles are boundless,
Through the meaning that is just as it is and uncontrived,
Settles the state with a single beat of the wings.
This is the certainty that cuts through
The doubts of hope and fear.
To cut through to the state of the view
Of the holy itself is most precious.

Friends, these should be understood as the discussion on cutting through to the ground completely.

When he had said this, those of lesser capacity attained the state of deciding without hope or fear.

This was the seventy-second chapter, called Cutting Through Doubt Completely, from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

Then again the Lord of Secrets spoke to those of lesser capacity: O friends, that by which experience is to be found is this: For entering the holy intention that gathers all, The chief thing is meditative concentration. Just as a universal monarch holds sway, Concentration holds sway over all phenomena. This great concentration of yoga, Like turning poison and darkness into gold, Is the concentration that holds sway over all. Therefore practice the holy concentration. If this concentration is absent, Like a boulder in the depths of the ocean, Though one has cut through countless doors, It is meaningless, its core dried up. The concentration of great yoga Is to rest the mind, uncontrived, In reality just as it is, Undistracted, without arising or ceasing. The holy yoga, just as it is, Is the very nature of mind itself. What cannot be observed, called suchness, Is the root all-knowing, the all-ground itself. Why? Because it is unmade and uncontrived, Self-arisen, it is called suchness. That which appears as everything Is the engaging consciousness, merely momentary. Why? Because it engages in appearing as everything, It is explained as appearing outside and inside. The array of causes Is the five doors of the engaging consciousness. Because it is set out as the cause of everything, It is called the cause from which all arises. The holy intention Is not empty, and has no permanence; In the middle, unobservable, all-knowing and clear, It transcends both existence and nonexistence. Base people who here pursue feeling, Those whose domain is the cessation of the childish, Those with perverse craving who have fallen from this, The faded and the slack, are one in having an object. Those here who are blinded by views, Relying on a self, establish extremes; Those inclined to nonexistence, thinking that beings do not exist, Go immediately to the lower realms. All those of slight devotion See selflessness as dependent arising. That which has stopped, with conception but no analysis, Is only the first concentration. From the subtle emptiness, all-empty, Abiding without conception but with analysis is the second. Settled with certainty in emptiness, With joy stirred up, is the third. Shown through the power of oneself or of another, Flawless and unmoving, is the abiding of the fourth. Whatever exists may change in the end, But what does not exist, without abiding, does not change in the end. Like what does not exist, it is not seen and not known; Though one seeks it, one will not find it, Yet that very reality does not become nonexistent; Though without cause and effect, it is not meaningless. For example, just as burned iron is without luster, And though the four great elements are absent and it becomes empty, Just as that and space are not nonexistent, So that very reality is not destroyed and is not nonexistent. The domain of the six is signs. Since signs appear as things, When left as they please, there is nothing to reverse; Their emptiness is abandoned and cannot be reversed. If, through the wordless view, one has decided That all is the very nature of mind, Uncontrived and undistracted, with distraction itself made even, Where is there effort, accomplishment, hope or fear? There is no need to cultivate antidotes to discordant factors. Faults and virtues, rejecting and adopting, dullness and agitation: If here they are indivisible and united, Calm abiding and insight arise from the mind. Through destruction, space does not become nonexistent; Through wandering, the mind of awakening is not lost; Through abandoning, boundless phenomena are not abandoned; Through washing, the expanse of emptiness is not cleansed. As for empty and existent, annihilation and permanence: Since all is awareness, it is not empty; Since it is without things, it is not permanent; Since it is nothing at all, there is nothing to practice. If all activities are entirely let go, If one rests there, uncontrived from the very beginning, Since that is absent, that too is absent. That is the finding of the holy experience. Here, in the holy Great Vehicle, The illness is not overcome in stages; The virulent poison of the gradualist Is that very reality which dawns all at once. Therefore, whoever they may be, According to how they understand and are inclined, Whatever teachings they follow, Let each make them an object of confidence. Thus the holy teachings, however many, Spoken by the immeasurable lamps, Are not settled as one, nor uncertain; As they arise, so they are certain. What cuts one off from entering this: Laziness, many activities, agitation, Distraction, torpor and desire, Sleep, hope and fear, doubt, The activity of gathering and radiating, and joy. Their nature, too, is suchness. The five poisons and the rest are the very origin; Self-arising and self-pacifying, they are suchness. What obscures the holy meaning: Unmoving stillness, clarity, bliss and suffering, Arising, clarity and bringing forth, Gentleness and pride in distinction: their arising and pacifying itself. What appears through these in great concentration Is never impaired, without hope or fear: Not producing faith, unmoving, Unattached, not cut off, without grasping; If there is no agitation, it is the intrinsic nature itself. The domain of the māras for the yogin: Seeing the holy before one, and speaking as the holy; Seeing countless emanations sent forth; Seeing clearly all that the holy ones say; Seeing clearly the knowledge of all times; All appearing by way of the six classes of beings; A surge of longing arising for that; Seeing all appearances clearly; Going unobstructed through anything; Moving and staying in the sky like a bird; Prophecy through the mudrā of the holy; Teaching the Dharma in the languages of all; Seeing things displayed, moving, and places of worship; Setting all out evenly; The clear appearance of that very nature; Pliancy and freedom from obstruction; Being exhorted toward the holy meaning; And offerings made by the lords of the world: If one knows their nature, they are signs of the holy; If one does not know, they are the māras of error. Whatever signs arise through the six doors, Self-arising and self-pacifying, leave them as they will. Since they are unborn, there is nothing to reverse; That too is suchness itself. Toward past, future and present, Toward any faults or good deeds, There are no thoughts of delight or regret: Self-arising and self-pacifying, that is reality itself. Here, whatever is certain of the holy Is the forerunner among the signs of yoga. Just as, wherever there are rivers, fire or gold, Their gathering, spreading, arising and essence are certain, If one is certain of the holy, it arises as the holy; If one is not certain of the holy, all is māra. If one is certain of the holy, even something small That arises is a forerunner of the holy.

Whoever hears of the holy itself
Is not afraid, is not terrified, and joy arises.
Whoever views the holy itself
Sees everything as the holy itself.
Whoever has entered the expanse of the holy
Gathers the many categories into the holy.
When one sees the holy with certainty,
It does not follow along the path of words.
Seeing it, all is clear as the holy.
The holy, which is without appearance,
Comes to see what has no appearance.
That itself is the cause of all,
And all the moving miraculous displays arise from it:
Movement, the attaining of warmth, and stability;
Clarity, the attaining of signs, and stability;
Sunbeams, motes of dust and mirages;
Ocean waves, wind and streams of rain;
Clouds hovering, rising up as high as a palm tree;
The earth moving, and the sense bases of the elements appearing;
The flow of the elements, piercing pains and the like.
That by which illness is wholly pacified,
The sense base of darkness is overcome,
Bodily forbearance is attained,
And all is at ease, just as it is;
Turning what is not into whatever one will,
And bringing forth whatever from what is not;
Finding pleasing, wish-fulfilling, supreme substances;
All delighting, all bowing, all pleasing to the mind,
And mastery over all: these are omens of supreme accomplishment.
Omens that come a moment beforehand:
Appearing naked and washing constantly,
Being carried off by pure rivers and swimming in them,
Streams of rain and vase water arising,
Scattering, dispersing and strewing about:
These are omens of the purity of whatever is being cleansed.
And here, being free from fear:
Battles subsiding and thorns drawn out,
Finally being released and freed from the executioner,
Being free of vicious hordes, poison and disease pacified,
Release from bonds and from captivity: these are good omens.
In particular, the forerunners of the holy:
Climbing the legs of a throne, trees and ladders,
And going upward;
Stūpas on mountain peaks and ladders to the sky:
Omens of rising higher and higher.
Signs of where one abides and what one mounts:
Sitting on a high throne, riding an elephant,
A great lion, a soaring garuḍa, or birds;
Sitting on the tops of stūpas and the crests of mountains,
And in the expanse of the sky;
Clouds emerging from one's body, and sitting on the sun and moon:
These are signs of where one abides.
The forerunners of the holy family:
Wielding authority over Indra with a seal of command,
Bringing the world down by a ladder,
Gathering power and being victorious in battle,
And flowers growing as a crown upon the head.
The forerunners of mastery over the Dharma:
Finding volumes, writing and using them,
Teaching them to others and establishing them;
Looking into a mirror or a full vase, examining and drinking;
Gathering jewels, and wheels descending;
Seizing the sun and moon, and circling Mount Meru;
Filling the trichiliocosm with a hundred sounds;
Seeing all things as letters;
Producing every wonder through one's renown;
And a rain of flowers falling from the sky.
The forerunners of seeing the holy:
Seeing lands one has not seen before,
Seeing the trichiliocosm and traversing the continents,
Reaching the summit of Mount Meru and seeing everything,
Seeing boundless space,
Reaching the depths of the ocean,
Holding a lamp and examining the world of appearance and existence:
They are like the very heart of the sun.
The forerunners of attaining the great transmission:
Music, thunder and lions,
Hearing discussion of the Dharma,
Images smiling and emitting light,
Hearing the speech of the holy ones but not seeing their bodies,
Goddesses proclaiming songs,
Sounds arising in celestial palaces,
And the words "holy friend" arising,
Which make one go on to that very reality.
When the omens of a holy vidyādhara dawn,
The miracles of the power of blessing as well:
Riding on the sun and moon and traveling,
Stirring Mount Meru from its waist,
Hurling the world far off,
Extinguishing the eon of burning,
And all such miracles
One is able to display with special prowess.
To all beings to be tamed,
In their own languages and in accord with the Dharma,
Through various doors of taming,
One is able to teach accordingly.
Through the special prowess of yoga,
Shaking all the world systems,
One makes all greatly amazed,
And they taste the flavor of delight and joy.
From garlands of golden letters,
By proclaiming the sound of the Dharma miraculously,
All the many categories of dharmas that the mind sees
Appear as mantra; none fails to become mantra.
The stages of greater and lesser yoga
Arise from distinctions in prowess.
Gradual entry and instantaneous entry:
The domain of breath, and feeling,
Hearing, seeing and the rest,
The domain of mind, and the expanse of space,
To the boundless limits of the expanse of phenomena;
The letters of the nature, the letters of transformation,
The letters of the limbs, each with its
Root and so on, together with a single basis;
The measure of radiating and gathering, the scope of the mudrās,
And the number of clusters likewise.
This is the first order of entry:
Becoming acquainted and habituated step by step.
The letters are created from primordial wisdom,
And accomplishing aims arises as mudrā:
Pressing down the ocean, ladders to the sky,
Binding the wind and turning back streams,
Extinguishing the eon of burning,
Riding the sun and moon and hurling the world,
Entering the earth and lifting Mount Meru,
Placing the trichiliocosm inside a mustard seed,
Demolishing, raising up and the rest:
What is thought of, inconceivable,
Is simply training in the great prowess of yoga
Made pliant through habituation.
Accomplishing recitation to its end,
Perfecting the binding completely,
Making one's intention pliant,
Seeing the meaning and accomplishing the words,
And activities that are completely pure:
The one meaning appears in distinct ways to its objects.
Therefore, although the special concentrations
Are cultivated on that one reality alone,
The distinctions in yogic prowess
Are that the objects of intention are various.
The yogin who abides on the first ground
Through the work of seeking,
In the six yogas, the yoga of the aspiring mind and the rest,
Since the reach of the prowess of wisdom is weak,
Has little prowess in concentration as well.
Since one does not come to see reality itself,
Whatever one's own inclination focuses on,
Whatever is its seed,
Distinguish it by color and shape,
Make it move with the breath of the nose,
And, to the extent that it has become its object,
From the clear blazing and radiating of light rays,
Generate the radiating and gathering at its place.
The extent of the body, matched to one's own measure, is that much.
The four yogas that abide on the second ground,
The yoga of the activity of the attendant and the rest,
Through the special prowess of concentration,
On the seed, the letter of the nature,
Together with its blazing light rays,
Up to the door from which smell arises,
Generate radiating and gathering clearly;
The extent of the body, twice one's own measure, is that much.

The three yogas that abide on the third ground,
The yoga of the command door of transmission and the rest,
Hold the absorption of focusing to be like this:
Whatever is the seed of the deity,
Transform it into the letter of the nature,
And with its blazing light rays,
Up to the range of hearing,
Make the radiating and gathering clear;
Then about four times one's own measure
Is the extent of the body.
Likewise, following the stages of yoga,
Whatever the distinctions of deity may be,
Apply them according to the order of the letters,
And the measure of radiating and gathering is likewise.
Applying them in their proper order,
When the radiating and gathering has become clear,
As far as the domain of the mind extends,
So far is the distinction in the extent of the body:
Since it reaches to the limits of the expanse of phenomena,
It is filled with the extent of the body.
Just as refined gold shines,
By refining mind itself, that very reality becomes clear.
This absorption of applying radiating and gathering
Is trained in gradually, while its warmth comes all at once.
Lord of Laṅkā, it is like this:
This great stream of the Dharma, the holding of it,
Was taught by the holy Vajrasattva.
All of this is called the stream of radiating and gathering.
On the volume of the meaning of space
Are written the letters of manifold knowledge.
Through the proclaiming of the sound of the four great elements,
The stream of the secret mantra of the Great Vehicle is unbroken.
The intrinsic nature of all phenomena
Has not moved from suchness,
So all is nectar, an unbroken stream.
The requisites of the ocean of saṃsāra
Have the nature of an ocean of offerings;
The whole boundless ocean of existence
Is an unbroken stream of Samantabhadra's offering clouds.
From the expanse that is the suchness of all,
Sentient beings are taught to be the essence of buddhahood.
Beings, inconceivable in number,
Are an unbroken stream of self-arisen buddhas.
The inconceivable sounds and languages
Of things stable and moving and all the rest
Are all the secret words of secret mantra,
An unbroken ocean of recitation.
In the very expanse of each and every thing,
Whatever means one applies,
Since from the beginning there is neither meeting nor parting,
The great union is unbroken.
These fields of the three realms of existence to be liberated,
Slain without substance
By the king of weapons, emptiness,
Are an unbroken great primordial liberation.
In the celestial palace of pure appearance and existence,
On the glorious maṇḍala of the all-ground,
With the eight threads of the great chalk lines of consciousness,
The maṇḍala laid out from the beginning is unbroken.
On Mount Meru, the foundation that is the desire realm,
The form realm is the terraces, the firm steps of the throne,
And the formless realm the vase base together with the vase:
The great reliquary, the dharmakāya, is unbroken.
Space, fire, water, earth and wind
Appear as the four foundations, the support of Mount Meru.
The terraces together with the seven mountains
Are its terraces, the firm steps of the throne;
The mountain is the vase base together with the vase.
This too is the great reliquary, unbroken.
The limbs of its construction: the support of the foundation,
The support of the joints of the wheels, the firm steps of the throne,
The first limb together with the vase base,
Covered with branches, crowns and garlands.
On the ground of suchness
Stands the celestial palace just as it is:
Its great foundation unmistaken, uncontrived and firm,
Filled in with the great walls of the mind of awakening,
With clear skylights of the five wisdoms,
The means of attraction and the immeasurables as the doors of liberation,
The eight stairs of right mindfulness,
And adorned with porticoes of miraculous power.
On the great beams of the fearless awarenesses,
It is beautified by the pillars of the eight right factors;
The pinnacle of self-arisen wisdom is exalted,
And it is surely beautified by the courtyard of the two truths.
From wisdom, compassion and good qualities
It is beautifully adorned with the ornaments of blessing;
The canopy of the waves of the nature of reality is stretched out,
And cymbals proclaim the sound of the mantra of the Great Vehicle.
In this palace of the Dharma just as it is,
The Victors reside in this order:
From the nature of the mind of Samantabhadra,
The all-ground, unmixed, the fully perfected Dharma;
Since the heroes attain certainty in an instant,
The ocean of the teaching is unbroken.
Having climbed the ladder of vajra space,
Abiding in the center of the expanse of space,
Look upon appearance and existence, which has no limit or end;
Through this, that domain becomes vast.
Having filled the ocean with vajras,
And having made Vajrasattva firm
In the beings who dwell there,
Make all into holy vajras.
On the emblem of the expanse of space,
Abiding in the center of the moon maṇḍala,
By the swift momentum of the moon in water,
One should travel to all the buddha fields.
On the emblem of the expanse of space,
Abiding in the center of the sun maṇḍala,
When it has become utterly certain,
All the places of darkness are illuminated.
On the tip of the yogin's threatening forefinger,
The clear light of the moon maṇḍala blazes;
When this is attained with great stability,
That water extinguishes the eon of burning.
In the maṇḍala of the yogin's hand,
The trichiliocosm with its encircling ring
Is hurled into the expanse of space,
And raised from place to place.
On the yogin's threatening forefinger,
From the clear sun maṇḍala,
A hundred thousand suns radiate from its rays,
Dispelling all outer and inner darkness.
From the maṇḍala of the yogin's hand,
The maṇḍala of the moon in water shines brightly;
A stream like a river of nectar comes forth
And quenches the great fires of the burning hells.

Moreover, Lord of Laṅkā, since it is taught that the sūtras of absorption are gathered in this alone, it should be understood exactly like this.

Mind is illusory by nature;
As it is imagined, so it appears.
Even if it does not happen at once,
Whatever it imagines, so it appears.
Though it does not appear in the domain of the material,
Mental form, higher perception and omniscience appear.
Once one has looked thus at whatever one imagines,
It comes to be clearly present to direct perception.
The natures of existence and nonexistence, good and bad,
Are simply like the element of water congealed.
All of them will be brought about by this.
Therefore in absorption, mind itself is chief.
For example, people who take jewels from the ocean
Face snakes, sea monsters, bees
And every suffering from vicious creatures;
Practicing with hardship, they succeed.
Likewise, yogins seeking ease
Face the enemies of conceptual thought, dullness and torpor, agitation and distraction;
If they put into practice
These deeds that are hard to conquer, they find and attain bliss.
This is the holy absorption,
The training put into practice.
From habituating oneself to mind itself,
Becoming familiar with it is most precious.

Friends, these should be understood as the very finding of experience.

When he had said this, those of lesser capacity found the experience and warmth of the holy intention.

This was the seventy-third chapter, called Mastery of Yoga, from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

Then again the Lord of Secrets spoke these words to the beings of lesser body: Friends, what is it that makes this effortless? It is this: having nothing at all to do. What is that in which there is nothing to do? It is this: suchness.

Why is that? It is like this: a disease that is wholly seized by mantra cannot harm anyone.

Those who are held by suchness
Are not harmed by the three poisons or the five poisons.
If one is not held by suchness,
Though one practices every factor of the path,
One will surely emerge only into the higher realms;
How could one reach the supreme state?
Whoever knows that suchness abides in all
Is fully liberated from the afflictions.
Whoever does not know that it is so held
Will not be liberated by the holy paths.
For the yogin who has insight into that very suchness,
Since the afflictions are held, whatever he does,
Liberation is self-arising, it is nirvāṇa,
Effortless, at ease, and of great meaning.
Whoever lacks this insight,
Whatever he does in the manner of the insightful,
Will not be liberated in the supreme sense.
Bondage, release, or the things of saṃsāra:
One with the insight that knows all of them as empty
Has passed into nirvāṇa,
Free of the exhausting deeds of activity,
Of great toil and hardship.
Without the means of holy insight,
Even for holy persons,
To take a lesser body for the sake of taming beings
Becomes a downfall.
If one has the means of holy insight,
Even the lowly things of saṃsāra
Are liberated into a holy person;
Liberation is self-arising, not from another.
Liberated, not liberated, and the like:
All conceptions of two are extremes.
The omniscient do not abide even in the middle.
That is liberation without liberation.
This is the supreme secret;
Keep it secret from all of small mind who do not realize it.
This is not because it is flawed or wrong,
But in order to benefit those of small mind.
Therefore, for those endowed with insight and means,
Exertion, practice, and implements were not taught as the main thing.
Even if they are done, there is no obstruction;
Even if they are not done, it is just so, with certainty.
Thus, if one is endowed with insight and means,
All sentient beings of the five deeds of immediate retribution,
Slain with the weapon of awakening and compassion,
Turn into immeasurable virtue. If they are not slain,
That which all pursue as their aim,
The very expanse of one's own mind,
Is not known, though one churn the three realms of existence
For countless aeons.
Here, for one endowed with insight and means,
Without action, at ease, through self-awareness,
The noble consort of the yoga of great meaning
Is never parted, and is spontaneously present.
Just as darkness, poison, and base substances
Are turned into light, medicine, and gold:
A person without the instructions for the elixir,
Though he performs many great and difficult deeds
And endures suffering, will not succeed;
But a person who has the instructions,
Effortlessly and at ease,
Transmutes them into medicine, light, and gold.
Just so the great yoga accomplishes its aim.
Those who lack that very insight and means,
Though they practice austerities, will not succeed.
Whoever sees this secret of the mind
Is endowed with insight and means;
Whatever supreme aims are desired,
These are accomplished effortlessly and at ease.
Whatever brings aims to accomplishment
Does so because it is accomplished by insight and means;
These trainings, by which aims come to be accomplished,
Are most precious.

Friends, these are called little effort and great meaning.

When he had said this, the lesser beings came to possess insight, were cut free by it, and were held by skillful means.

This was the seventy-fourth chapter, called Definitive Accomplishment of the Meaning, from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

Then again the Lord of Secrets called out in a most majestic manner to the beings of lesser body, the Lord of Laṅkā and the rest, and said: O friends, what is the sūtra condensed into one? It is this: Thus did I hear at one time. The Blessed One of all the tathāgatas, having gathered all the tathāgatas so that they became the purity of essential nature, explained how all the tathāgatas are gathered into a single intention:

Because the nature of reality is completely pure,
All phenomena are completely pure by intrinsic nature.
Of all the tathāgatas
It is the very supreme treasure of the heart commitment.

Thus it comes from the Blessed One, the purity of essential nature.

Moreover, it is like this:

The indestructible vajra commitment
Arises from vajra suchness.
The vajra commitment of the nature of reality,
Once accomplished, knows no time of destruction.

Friends, thus spoke the Blessed One, from the completely pure essential nature.

Again, friends, it is like this:

The heart commitment of all the victors,
From that very supreme of all,
Is not other; there is no other; it is not existent;
Nothing whatsoever passes beyond it.

Thus spoke the Blessed One, the pure essential nature of perfect enjoyment.

Again, friends, it is like this:

The word spoken by the holy ones,
The vajra armor that was vowed,
This most sacred oath:
If one transgresses it, one will burn.

Thus spoke the Blessed One, the pure essential nature of the secret body.

Again, friends, it is like this:

This sacred word of the victors,
Having been spoken, all the endless commitments,
Each and every one, are kept;
Such keeping is hard to find.

Thus spoke the Blessed One, the pure essential nature of continuous arising.

O friends, it is like this:

Aversion, distinct in its essence,
Being equal as the mind of awakening,
Is other than suchness:
Unabandoned, unpurified, the vajra commitment.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajrasattva, pure in essential nature.

Friends, it is like this:

The very objects of desire and attachment,
Whatever their intrinsic nature, are suchness.
Enjoyment, experience, and feeling are completely pure;
Unattached in conduct: the vajra commitment.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Amitābha, pure in essential nature.

Again, it is like this:

Since all are alike, equal in that suchness,
Without distinction, particularity, or division,
Through the luminosity of vajra space
The black darkness of delusion is purified and cleared.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Vairocana, pure in essential nature.

Again, it is like this:

Pure, without limit or boundary,
Free of the verbal extremes of wide and narrow,
One in Samantabhadra's vast path,
The expanse of great bliss is also the vajra commitment.

Thus spoke the Blessed One, the All-Accomplished.

Again, it is like this:

Since there is nothing apart from suchness,
It is beyond the two of is and is not.
Selflessness is vajra suchness;
Without an I, unchanging: the vajra commitment.

Thus spoke the Blessed One, the pure essential nature of the source of all qualities.

Friends, furthermore, it is like this:

All phenomena, whatever exist, are seen, appear, or are heard,
Though distinct, various, and endless,
Are by intrinsic nature unfabricated suchness,
The unobscured, luminous, pure realm.

Thus spoke the Blessed One, the pure essential nature of stainless sound.

Likewise, it is like this:

This wisdom of Samantabhadra
Is made by no one; it is self-arising.
Since it is the root from which all arises,
The maṇḍala that arises from itself is complete.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Jewel Cloud, pure in essential nature.

Furthermore, the explanation of the intention is this:

Primordially unfabricated, just as it is,
It is the essence of all phenomena.
From what does not exist, various appearances arise;
Without appearance, the maṇḍala of suchness is complete.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Source of Arrays, pure in essential nature.

Furthermore, it is like this:

Through the very mind of self-arisen awareness,
The meaning of the unfabricated expanse of the nature of reality,
From spontaneously present means and wisdom,
The maṇḍala of the true intrinsic nature shines clear.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Lion's Roar, pure in essential nature.

Furthermore, the Blessed One Beautiful Crest, pure in essential nature, said:

All the kinds of pleasure and displeasure
Of the world and of what transcends the world,
Being of one taste in suchness,
There is no feeling of pleasure or pain whatsoever.

Furthermore, the Blessed One Beautiful Ornament, pure in essential nature, said this:

To teach the meaning as suchness:
Since all are one in suchness,
Who teaches what to whom?
It is nothing but itself speaking of itself.

Furthermore, the Blessed One King, pure in essential nature, said this:

In the very expanse of suchness,
Three times or four times do not apply.
Through the distinction of wisdom awareness,
An instant is an aeon, and that aeon an instant.

Furthermore, the Blessed One King of Compassion, pure in essential nature, said this:

Saṃsāra is forever nirvāṇa
In the very expanse of suchness.
Since it is primordially beyond sorrow,
The two accumulations are by nature spontaneously complete.

Again, the Blessed One, the purity of essential nature, said this:

As for the intrinsic nature of all phenomena,
From the unborn, everything is born;
In being born itself, there is no birth:
The supreme family, the sacred vajra place of birth.

Again, the Blessed One King of Complete Intention, pure in essential nature, said this:

If one knows all as suchness,
Wherever it pervades is a buddha field.
Arrays of clouds of precious qualities:
There is nothing whatever to be arranged.

Again, the Blessed One Golden Light Rays, pure in essential nature, said this:

The immeasurable victors
Abide in suchness, just as it is.
Suchness and all things as well
Are nondual: that very suchness.

Again, the Blessed One Glorious Light, pure in essential nature, said this:

Saṃsāra and nirvāṇa
Do not appear as separate.
Since it is suchness, just as it is,
In it there is no nature of saṃsāra.

Again, the Blessed One Lord of Peace, pure in essential nature, said thus:

The very reality of suchness,
Being boundless to estimate and measure,
Is the palace of the immeasurable king.
From there, all is arrayed, being one in it.

Again, the Blessed One Glorious Cloud, pure in essential nature, said this:

Even the miraculous display from which all arises
Is wisdom that turns into anything at all.
Since there are no material phenomena,
It arises just as it is focused upon.

Again, the Blessed One Pleasing Melody, pure in essential nature, said this:

Through the continuous chain of the world's saṃsāra,
The essence of ignorance, and likewise the I,
Is a groundless, rootless instant.
If one knows that very thing, one abides in great bliss.

Again, the Blessed One Mirror Light, pure in essential nature, said this:

To conceive of the afflictions as the five wisdoms:
Both of these obscure it as it is.
Conceiving and not conceiving are not two;
That very conception is the true path.

Again, the Blessed One Glorious Moonlight, pure in essential nature, said this:

Even the teaching of countless vehicles,
According to people's differing inclinations,
Is merely a means of guiding them;
There is nothing but the Vajra Vehicle itself.

Again, the Blessed One Glorious Sun Essence, pure in essential nature, said this:

Whoever sees that, the moment he sees it,
Cuts through the net of unknowing,
Just like the garuḍa and the lion,
Though veiled by the womb of the body as by a womb.

Again, the Blessed One King of Fragrance, pure in essential nature, said this:

Through the wisdom of the means of mastery,
Having gained mastery over illusory phenomena,
Just as light arises in darkness,
Mastery over all is self-awareness.

Again, the Blessed One King of Former Joy, pure in essential nature, said this:

The illusion of pure wisdom
And the illusion of wrong conception and signs, these two:
Since their intrinsic nature is pure as that very reality,
The very knowing of them as two completes the accumulations.

Again, the Blessed One Jewel King, pure in essential nature, said this:

Since these inconceivable phenomena
Are one in suchness,
Whoever knows that suchness
Has attained mastery over all.

Again, the Blessed One Glorious Light Rays, pure in essential nature, said this:

Whoever, having understood this intention of the buddhas,
Abides in it,
Having gained mastery over self-awareness itself,
Is likewise truly a vidyādhara.

Again, the Blessed One King of Austerity, pure in essential nature, said this:

When that very reality becomes manifest, just as it is,
The stream of great qualities,
In which the inexhaustible arises from nothing, is never broken:
A treasure of jewels from which arises whatever appears.

Again, the Blessed One Uṣṇīṣa Essence, pure in essential nature, said this:

In that very reality, unfabricated just as it is,
Abides the mind that is suchlike.
Where could there be accomplishing and things to accomplish
That are not that very suchness?

Again, the Blessed One Definitively Gone, pure in essential nature, said this:

Just as clouds suddenly gather in the sky,
So it is veiled by sudden thoughts.
Just as clouds clear within the pure sky,
When concepts clear, it clears of itself.

Again, the Blessed One Definitively Spoken, pure in essential nature, said this:

However many pure utterances are spoken,
In vajra speech there is no elaboration.
Unelaborated speech itself clearly pervades all;
The very unspoken is explained at length.

Again, the Blessed One, the purity of essential nature, said this:

With the unobstructed, unattached, suchlike mind
That engages all objects,
All things, as many as there are, are transformed into suchness itself:
A jewel arises that holds an inexhaustible treasure.

Again, the Blessed One Melodious Lord of Flowers, pure in essential nature, said this:

Since they abide in suchness itself,
Although they do not say "this" in words,
Through the blessing of oneness that pervades all,
Each meets them just as he wishes.

Again, the Blessed One Glorious Renown, pure in essential nature, said this:

Buddhas, sentient beings, and the rest,
All without exception, with none left over,
Self-awareness, ignorance, and conceptuality itself:
All of them are self-arisen wisdom.

Again, the Blessed One King of Emanation, pure in essential nature, said this:

Letters, mudrās, and wisdom too
Are the ground from which words arise.
Since that very reality is pure, all is pure;
Speech does not abide in speech itself.

Again, the Blessed One Inexhaustible Melodious Sound, pure, said this:

Suchness too, by its intrinsic nature,
Is the complete purity of the five aggregates and the afflictions:
Free of the dust of concepts, without arising or ceasing,
All are vanquished in essential nature.

Again, the Blessed One King of Medicine, pure in essential nature, said this:

In immeasurable worlds as many as atoms,
Among inconceivable beings as numerous as particles,
Suchness appears in each particular,
Abiding as oil pervades the sesame seed.

Again, the Blessed One Radiant Golden Light, pure in essential nature, said this:

Intending a person as small as a single particle,
He utters speech before him;
Yet immeasurable, boundless beings, as many as atoms,
All of them, are liberated by hearing it.

Again, the Blessed One King of Arrays, pure in essential nature, said this:

One speech is inexhaustible, boundless speech;
Without edge or center, it pervades all.
Pervading and not pervading: vajra speech.
The various are without entity: suchness.

Again, the Blessed One Golden Speech, pure in essential nature, said this:

In that very reality, not fabricated at all,
There is no direction, no before and after, no time.
All is the expanse of suchness;
That very reality is self-awareness, itself.

Again, the Blessed One Glorious King, pure in essential nature, said this:

That very reality is the nature of all those things;
Though all those things are that very reality,
The childish grasp them as two;
In it there is nothing to be divided into two.

Again, the Blessed One Glorious Heaped Light, pure in essential nature, said this:

In emptiness, whose nature is space,
The adverse factors and the antidotes, these two,
Are primordially indivisible and one;
So how could there be fear of harm?

Again, the Blessed One Glorious Purity of Essential Nature, pure in essential nature, said this:

It is the unchanging nature of space.
To the pure, it is purity itself;
To the conceptual, it appears as concepts.
It appears as whatever will tame; in itself there is nothing at all.

Again, the Blessed One Glorious Supreme Joy, pure in essential nature, said this:

Intrinsic nature, unfabricated by itself,
Luminous of itself, is suchness.
Since that is the essence of all,
It is the great space obscured by nothing.

Again, the Blessed One Glorious Splendor, pure in essential nature, said this:

Suchness itself is the supreme ground.
Through the supreme wisdom that knows it,
One abides primordially in suchness;
How could there be anything left to traverse now?

Again, the Blessed One Spiritual Friend of All Buddhas, pure in essential nature, said this:

The essence of emptiness
Is surrounded by a retinue of various appearances;
Made by no one, self-arising,
It is the very maṇḍala of things just as they are.

So he spoke, in secret words.

Again, the Blessed One Purifier of All Lower Realms, pure in essential nature, said this in secret:

The accomplishment of inexhaustible wisdom,
Being accomplished as suchness,
Neither diminishes nor increases;
How could there be anything left to accomplish now?

So he spoke, in secret words.

Again, the Blessed One of All Worlds, pure in essential nature, said this:

The very absence of intrinsic nature in all phenomena
Is what is called nirvāṇa.
In emptiness, whose nature is completely pure,
Not abiding even in that: this is the mind of awakening.

So he spoke, in secret words.

Again, the Blessed One Completely Victorious, pure, said this:

Concepts are the headwaters of a channel;
If they are not turned into the field of emptiness,
They become the distracted ocean of saṃsāra.
To one who does not know this, it appears that way.

So he spoke, in secret words.

Again, the Blessed One King of Flower Arrays, pure in essential nature, said this:

Suchness, just as it is,
Is the mother from which all arises:
Samantabhadra, the perfection of wisdom,
The abode of all sentient beings and buddhas.

So he spoke, in secret words.

Again, the Blessed One All-Illuminating, pure in essential nature, said this:

The buddhas of Ghanavyūha
And sentient beings of every kind,
Things stable and moving, and all the rest,
Arise from that very reality: the body without entity.

So he spoke, in secret words.

Again, the Blessed One Mind, pure in essential nature, said this:

Since the root of self-arisen wisdom
Is the mind of awakening itself,
Its root is compassion,
Brought to perfection through means.

So he spoke, in secret words.

Again, the Blessed One Lion of a Hundred Thousand Forms, pure in essential nature, said this:

The meaning of suchness in all its multiplicity
Is indicated by letters, collections of names, and sounds.
Without relying on names and sounds,
There is no knowing it through the mind of experience and warmth.

So he spoke, in secret words.

Again, the Blessed One Lord of Sages, pure in essential nature, said this:

The nature of all phenomena, of every dharma,
Is not other than suchness;
Therefore that itself is the path of awakening,
And that path is the abode of Ghanavyūha.

So he spoke, in secret words.

O friends, furthermore, these and others, the pure essential natures of the emanated lamps of Vajrasattva, as many as tens of millions and millions, and the pure essential natures of the boundless ones who dwell in the palace of the Dharma, spoke these secret words thus. Some spoke like this:

The nonconceptual, self-aware instant
Is the expanse of both sentient beings and buddhas.
Merely the two, awareness and unawareness,
Appear as the two, pure and impure.

Thus they extolled it as the supreme secret. Some spoke like this:

That very reality is beyond speech and expression;
In order to indicate it, distinctions are spoken.
Whatever is spoken is suchness itself;
That very reality is the unelaborated vajra meaning.

Thus they extolled it as the supreme secret. Some spoke like this:

When that very reality does not appear as anything at all,
It is merely those very concepts,
The adventitious and differing concepts, that appear.
In appearance itself there is no appearance.

Thus they extolled it as the supreme secret.

Some spoke like this:

If even the buddhas cannot make
The reality that is just as it is,
By what was it fabricated, and who made it?
How could there be any maker?

Thus they extolled it as the supreme secret. Some spoke like this:

Since the domain of those who have wisdom
Is that very suchness,
All phenomena pervaded by it,
Pure by intrinsic nature, are unobscured.

Thus they extolled it as the supreme secret. Some spoke like this:

From that very reality without characteristics,
Various characteristics appear;
Being of the nature of the expanse from which all arises,
It is a treasury, nonexistent yet inexhaustible.

Thus they extolled it as the supreme secret. Some spoke like this:

The views of the various vehicles
And all the activities of guarding vows,
Though they are not things to be practiced,
Are all perfectly complete, unmixed.

Thus they extolled it as the supreme secret. Some spoke like this:

In it there is no direction and no time;
To whoever imagines directions and times,
That very reality appears in those forms.
The buddhas do not know it so.

Thus they extolled it as the supreme secret. Some spoke like this:

Having known all that is inconceivable,
If one rests it without thinking,
Immeasurable qualities arise:
This is omniscience.

Thus they extolled it as the supreme secret. Some spoke like this:

Whoever, not knowing the inconceivable,
Rests in a state without thought,
That childish one is a fool
And becomes the very sense base of darkness.

Thus they extolled it with supreme, all-embracing love. Some spoke like this:

Those who, having seen with insight
The unthinkable, inconceivable phenomena,
Abide in calm and make concepts
Of rejecting and adopting: their obscurations are boundless.

Thus they advised as supreme spiritual friends. Some spoke like this:

Though one knows all the various things individually,
If one lacks the completely pure path of total liberation,
One strays far from the supreme itself
And never finds certainty.

Thus they advised with a supreme command. Some spoke like this:

The various gradual approaches
And thought-based conduct remain within the intellect.
To the meaning of thought-free total perfection,
The gradual approach is a great poison.

Thus they advised with supreme distinction. Some spoke like this:

Without knowing the various things individually,
One does not find certainty in suchness.
If one has not found certainty in that very reality,
It is not fitting to speak of the various.

Thus they advised with supreme earnestness. Some spoke like this:

Just as a rainbow appears in the sky,
So the inconceivable appears as thought.
The speech that is not fixed as one
Is just as it arises.

Thus they spoke of it as the supreme that is hard to fathom. Some spoke like this:

Since the root of all is explained here,
Whoever knows its meaning has the supreme of learning.
Since all arises from this,
To know this is to know all: supreme wisdom.

Thus they extolled it as supreme wisdom. Some spoke like this:

This great secret within the secret
Does not see even itself by itself;
How could it be seen by another?
That very reality is extolled by the holy ones.

Thus they extolled it as the supreme secret. Some spoke like this:

This essence of all phenomena
Is unchanging, like gold and like space;
Because it neither sets, nor is emptied, nor is parted,
It is the supreme of the Dharma, the highest meaning.

Thus they extolled it as the great supreme. Some spoke like this:

The immeasurable banks of the clouds of concepts,
Accumulated from beginningless time,
Are completely released into suchness
By the instantaneous awareness of emptiness.

Thus they extolled it as the supreme secret. Some spoke like this:

If one knows with certainty that all phenomena,
However many, inconceivable, are empty,
This union equal with all the buddhas
Is the meditative concentration of great yoga.

Thus they advised it as the supreme yoga. Some spoke like this:

For as long as saṃsāra exists,
The sun of the essence of the teaching has risen;
This wheel of intrinsic nature, the nature of reality,
Turns without setting, without interruption.

Thus they extolled it as the vehicle of the supreme secret. Some spoke like this:

The vajra meaning, unchanging and indestructible;
The jewel from which all the various things arise;
The lotus itself, unattached and completely pure;
The great activity that accomplishes all spontaneously;
Suchness, unmistaken and unfabricated.

Thus again they taught it as supreme certainty. Some spoke like this:

Emptiness, the formless body of reality;
Wishlessness, the speech beyond the mind;
Signlessness, the heart beyond concepts;
Nonconceptual, the wish-fulfilling treasury of qualities;
The dance of means, the treasury of enlightened activity.

Thus they extolled it as the arising of various miracles. Some spoke like this:

The clear mirror of nonconceptual Dharma;
All equal, because nondual;
Knowing the various characteristics unmixed;
Great diligence that accomplishes all-pervasively;
Knowing the nature of all as primordial itself.

Thus they extolled it as wisdom dawning continuously. Some spoke like this:

Space that opens room for appearance and emptiness, pervading all;
Earth, on which all abide in the expanse of the vehicle;
The great water of completely pure calm abiding;
The fire blazing with the light of insight's wisdom;
The wind that scatters the knowable without obstruction.

Thus they extolled it as that from which all arises. Some spoke like this:

Form, unmixed, totally complete, clear and empty;
Sound, the voice of beings, pervasive and clear by nature;
The fragrance of the Dharma, refreshing with its clarity;
The taste of experiencing the ten meanings;
The very touch of the meaning of supreme bliss.

Thus they extolled it as supreme qualities. Some spoke like this:

Delusion, which thinks of nothing at all;
Aversion itself, which vanquishes all things;
Pride, the great self, primordially all-pervading;
Passion, whose nature is great bliss;
Jealousy, never turning into anything other.

Thus they extolled it as being without adopting or rejecting. Some spoke like this:

By knowing the one nature of all,
Everything gathers under that very power;
Since one attains mastery over all,
The great primordial empowerment is complete.

Thus they extolled it as the supreme empowerment. Some spoke like this:

The sūtra of awareness in which all is gathered,
Abiding pervasively in all, hard to examine,
Is the profound domain of the buddhas,
The yoga scripture of the Vajra Array.

Thus they extolled it as the supreme word. Some spoke like this:

Since all are one in that very reality,
Nothing appears as self and other.
In the completely pure space of emptiness,
There is not even a speck of distinction between clean and foul.

Thus they extolled it as the supreme nectar. Some spoke like this:

On the supreme heart of awakening,
How could the dust of concepts settle?
Being encompassed within the great reality,
It is moved by nothing and is unafraid.

Thus they extolled it as being without hope and fear. Some spoke like this:

This mountain of concepts and signs
Is like a lion's carcass, which decays only from within:
If emptiness itself does not destroy it,
How could it be destroyed by another?

Thus they extolled it as the supreme intrinsic nature.

Some spoke like this:

Those who conceptualize completely pure great bliss
Conceive of both good and bad.
When one has entered the wind of definitive great bliss,
Here there is no conceptualization.

Thus they extolled it as beyond all conceptualization. Some spoke like this:

That very reality, unerringly:
What is to be guarded is not to conceptualize and not to abide.
Guard against the enemies, laziness and distraction,
And likewise do not transgress it.

Thus they extolled it as the supreme commitment. Some spoke like this:

In immeasurable worlds,
Through inconceivable lamps,
The Dharma taught in all its multiplicity,
With its boundless meanings, is gathered into one.

Thus they extolled it. Some spoke like this:

If one does not stray from that very reality,
Moral discipline, meditative absorption, and wisdom,
Free of memory, thought, and illusion,
The three trainings are perfectly complete.

Thus they extolled it as the supreme training. Some spoke like this:

When, from that very reality, the three collections,
In which final calm abiding is mingled
With the seeing of the higher meaning, are definitively complete,
Even boundless hundreds of thousands cannot express it.

Thus they extolled it as the supreme lineage of guidance. Some spoke like this:

If one transforms words through mastery over words,
Though words are endless, when condensed they are gathered;
If a single word is elaborated,
It will not be exhausted by speaking.

Thus they extolled it as supreme inexhaustible speech. Some spoke like this:

Since they are pure in speech, expression, and word,
Their intention cannot be fathomed by anyone.
To hear an ocean of collections of words is not hearing;
To hear a single word is the supreme of hearing.

Thus they extolled it as the supreme of an ocean of learning. Some spoke like this:

Pervading and not abiding, nonconceptual,
Never parted, unmoving, unattached:
There is nothing other than that very reality;
That alone is the perfection that has gone beyond.

Thus they extolled it as the supreme that has reached the end. Some spoke like this:

To recite, utter, inquire, and read for the sake of meaning
Is like a blind man running headlong.
Whoever knows the meaning written by the expanse upon the expanse
Has the supreme of recitation, utterance, inquiry, and reading.

Thus they extolled it as supreme conduct. Some spoke like this:

What is made into signs, and the supreme of appearances,
Unmixed and perfectly complete,
Known to be like space and like a rainbow:
Unsurpassed dedication is awakening itself.

Thus they extolled it as the supreme dedication. Some spoke like this:

Whoever, in the reality of mind of any phenomenon,
Views duality or extremes
Is inverted through the delusion of ignorance
And has turned away from the path of the Great Vehicle's secret mantra.

Thus they spoke it as the supreme instruction. Some spoke like this:

Causes, conditions, and dependent origination in stages
Are means for guiding the small-minded and deluded.
In this unfabricated suchness
There is no dependent origination, no cause and effect, and no stages.

Thus they extolled it as supreme certainty. Some spoke like this:

In this unfabricated suchness,
Through the arising of miracles
That, like a wish-fulfilling gem, arise as anything at all,
Sentient beings see, hear, and perceive whatever will tame them.

Thus they extolled all as being without cause. Some spoke like this:

The profound domain of the buddhas,
The very essence of the secret of all
That abides in the secret expanse:
By seeing it, the benefit of beings is perfected like an ocean.

Thus they extolled all as spontaneously present. Some spoke like this:

The essential nature that is without entity:
Seeing it, no essential nature is observed.
Without memory, without act or agent,
This is the recollection of the equality of the three spheres.

Thus they extolled it as the supreme meditative absorption. Some spoke like this:

Without the perception that focuses on the three spheres,
Nameless, free from the path of words,
The unsurpassed, unconditioned offering
Is declared the true and supreme offering.

Thus they extolled it as the supreme offering. Some spoke like this:

The three darknesses of perversity and clinging,
Over countless tens of millions of aeons,
Are entirely cleared by that very reality as it truly is,
By a single all-illuminating instant.

Thus they extolled it as the supreme secret. Some spoke like this:

Those who are enveloped in darkness
Should look upon that very reality, unmoving and nonconceptual.
Seeing it, the darkness is completely purified and cleared;
This is declared the supreme of all confession and purification.

Thus they extolled it as supreme purity. Some spoke like this:

Those who abide in the nature of reality, emptiness,
In which adverse factors and antidotes are not observed,
Have no antidote at all
To teach.

Thus they extolled it as the supreme resolution. Some spoke like this:

All things are by nature unfabricated;
Just so, as they are,
To generate the mind toward the supreme awakening
Of unsurpassed self-awareness.

Thus they extolled the generation of the mind. Some spoke like this:

Thinking of nothing at all, unmoving,
Nonconceptual and undistracted,
The expanse spread out, pervading all,
Accommodating all, without action: the supreme Great Vehicle.

Thus they extolled it as the supreme secret of the Great Vehicle. Some spoke like this:

This essence of the secret of the Great Vehicle
Is not observed as a middle and transcends the extremes.
Those here who hold to a middle,
How could they transcend the extremes?

Thus they extolled it as observing neither middle nor extremes. Some spoke like this:

Not seeing any phenomena, not seeing
Even the nature of reality, not seeing the minds of sentient beings,
Not seeing the buddhas: whoever sees in this way
Truly sees its ultimate meaning.

Thus they extolled it as complete seeing. Some spoke like this:

Primordially unfabricated, just as it is,
Unborn, unceasing, knowing all aspects,
Made by no one, self-arisen:
Conceptual thoughts are adventitious.

Thus they extolled it as supreme and unadulterated. Some spoke like this:

Whoever possesses that very reality
Is a buddha, even in a lesser body.
Apart from that, where
Could there be anything called a buddha?

Thus they extolled it as primordial awakening. Some spoke like this:

The stable and the moving are the complete maṇḍala and mudrā;
Thought and grasping, and the limits of sound, are meditative absorption and mantra;
What does not exist, causes, signs, and attachments are words that indicate;
Accomplishing and the accomplished, looking and appearance, are the result.

Thus are the supreme words of secret mantra.

Friends, in this way, from that very reality, the supreme intention of all the tathāgatas, this was spoken:

All, everything, however much there is,
Is nothing but the conventions of words.
In this there is nothing at all to do;
That very nature is primordially awakened.

a ho With this, all passed beyond the tathāgatas and became great bliss, spontaneous and clear.

Then the Lord of Secrets himself spoke in verse:

For example, a person who wanders everywhere
Goes to whatever land he desires;
Though the kinds of land are endless,
The destination, the path, and the abode are only one.
Likewise, however many inconceivable
Enumerations and doors of Dharma there may be,
They are words that lead by guiding;
The object of the intention is one.

The meanings gathered into one
Are the sublime thing to be trained in;
Because, definitively, there is nothing but one,
The sublime training that gathers all is precious.
If one knows all as gathered in this sublime one,
That person's wisdom is limitless;
Because he is omniscient in all aspects,
The meaning of the sūtras is taught as gathered into one.

Friends, these things were spoken by all the tathāgatas, gathered together, so that the intention of all the tathāgatas is gathered into the one suchness. You should know that all the sūtras are gathered into suchness.

When he had said this, the lowly ones found the sublime certainty of oneness. Then the lowly ones rejoiced in the Lord of Secrets himself and praised him:

How wonderful! Great being, Lord of Secrets,
Great in prowess, lion's roar of speech,
Heart of all the tathāgatas,
Arisen primordially, before all buddhas!
You are the forefather of all buddhas,
Greatest and supreme, venerable, sublime body,
Supreme spiritual friend of all the sugatas,
The very reality of all bodhisattvas and buddhas,
Source of all, clear mirror,
Definitive sūtra clarifying and explaining every direction,
Great Vehicle of wondrous renown, how marvelous!
The way the great secret arose, how marvelous!

Crying "a ho!", they praised the sublime certainty. Then nothing at all remained perceptible, and the sublime secrets that had been definitively discussed there became widely renowned and universally renowned throughout the worlds of gods and humans.

This is the seventy-fifth chapter, called "The Gathering of the Intention of All Buddhas," from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

From the definitive emergence of the Great Vehicle: the discussion of the wisdom of the Dharma seal, which relates the account of what also occurred when the intention of each was discussed. This is the conclusion of the twelfth section, which teaches the manner of occurrence.

The Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas, the clear realization of the Great Vehicle, the mirror of all phenomena, the commentary on all the Buddha's words, the sublime secret intention of all the tathāgatas, discussed at length by the Blessed One, the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi, on the summit of the fierce Mount Malaya, to the Lord of Laṅkā and the other beings of lowly body, was translated and finalized from the Bruzha script, in the town of the land of Bruzha, by the Indian preceptor Dharmabodhi, the great master Dānarakṣita, and the chief editor-translator Ché Tsenkyé.

This scripture contains, from beginning to end, twelve sections on the manner of occurrence, seventy-five chapters, ten fascicles, and two hundred and fifty-two ślokas.

ye dharmā hetu prabhabā hetunteṣān tathāgato hyabadad teṣāñca yo nirodha evaṃ bādī mahāśramaṇaḥ

Translator's notes

The Drusha (Burushaski) title is given as a plain sound-rendering of the Tibetan spelling. The sentence on the Lord of Secrets near the beginning is long and loosely constructed; it is taken as describing the setting in which the teaching is heard and expounded across all realms. The phrase rendered "after the teacher has passed into complete nirvāṇa" could also be read as the bodhisattvas themselves having passed beyond.
